Still scowling, the Butcher took a white soapy crayon from the bulging pocket in his silver shorts. Pressed into the pavement, it made a black mark. He scrawled pensively: **Keep on the grass**.
He gazed at his handiwork. No, darn it, that was just what grownups wanted you to do. This grass couldn’t be hurt. You couldn’t pull it up or tear it off; it hurt your fingers to try. A rub with the side of the crayon removed the sign. He thought for a moment, then wrote: **Keep off the grass**.
With an untroubled countenance, he sprang up and hurried after the others.
Joggy and the older boy were swimming lazily through the air at shoulder height. In the pavement directly under each of them was a wide, saucer-shaped depression which swam along with them. The uninjes avoided the depressions. Darter was strutting on his hind legs, looking up inquiringly at his master.
“Gimme a ride, Hal, gimme a ride!” the Butcher called. The older boy ignored him. “Aw, gimme a ride, Joggy.”
“Oh, all right.” Joggy touched the small box attached to the front of his broad metal harness and dropped lightly to the ground. The Butcher climbed on his back. There was a moment of rocking and pitching, during which each boy accused the other of trying to upset them.
Then the Butcher got his balance and they began to swim along securely, though at a level several inches lower. Brute sprang up after his master and was invisibly rebuffed. He retired baffled, but a few minutes later, he was amusing himself by furious futile efforts to climb the hemispherical repulsor field.
Slowly the little cavalcade of boys and uninjes proceeded down the Avenue of Wisdom. Hal amused himself by stroking toward a tree. When he was about four feet from it, he was gently bounced away.
---
It was really a more tiring method of transportation than walking and quite useless against the wind. True, by rocking the repulsor hemisphere backward, you could get a brief forward push, but it would be nullified when you rocked forward. A slow swimming stroke was the simplest way to make progress.

===Chinese===

大男孩耸耸肩说：“哦，行吧——怎么样，‘屠夫’？”
屠夫猛地转身。“他们不让我进时间剧场。你自己说的。”
“你可以送我们过去呀。”
“这个嘛，兴许送，兴许不送。”
“你慢慢想吧，我们先游起来。走吧，乔吉。”
屠夫还沉着脸，从银色短裤鼓鼓的口袋里掣出一支肥皂似的白色蜡笔。往路面上一按，划出黑道道。他若有所思地涂写：**请在草地上行走**。
他端详自己的大作。不，见鬼，这恰恰是大人们要你做的。这草地伤不了。你拔不起它，也撕不下它，去掰还硌手。蜡笔侧面一抹，字迹没了。他想了想，写：**请勿践踏草地**。
他一脸无事，蹦起来追上其他人。
乔吉和大男孩正齐肩高的空中懒洋洋地游着。各自正下方的路面里有一片碟形的凹坑，随人一起游动。无伤犬们绕开凹坑。飞镖用后腿昂首走着，仰头询问地看主人。
“驮我一段，哈尔，驮我一段！”屠夫喊。大男孩不理他。“唉，驮我一段嘛，乔吉。”
“哦，行吧。”乔吉碰了碰宽金属背带前的小盒子，轻轻落到地上。屠夫爬上他后背。一阵摇晃颠簸，两人互相指责对方想把他们掀翻。
随后屠夫找回了平衡，两人稳稳地游起来，只是低了那么几英寸。蛮牛朝主人一跃，被无形的力场挡了回去。它败兴地退下，可几分钟后，又对着那半球形的排斥场拼命往上爬，自得其乐地白费劲。
男孩和无伤犬的小小队伍沿智慧大道缓缓前行。哈尔拿朝一棵树划水取乐。离树约四英尺时，他被轻轻弹开。
---
这其实是一种比走路还累的移动方式，顶风时全然无用。诚然，把排斥半球朝后一摇，能换来一股向前的短促推力，可朝前一摇又抵消了。慢速的划水是前进最省事的法子。

===Original===

The general sensation, however, was delightful and levitators were among the most prized of toys.
“There’s the Theater,” Joggy announced.
“I *know*,” the Butcher said irritably.
But even he sounded a little solemn and subdued. From the Great Ramp to the topmost airy finial, the Time Theater was the dream of a god realized in unearthly substance. It imparted the aura of demigods to the adults drifting up and down the ramp.
“My father remembers when there wasn’t a Time Theater,” Hal said softly as he scanned the façade’s glowing charts and maps. “Say, they’re viewing Earth, somewhere in Scandinavia around zero in the BC-AD time scale. It should be interesting.”
“Will it be about Napoleon?” the Butcher asked eagerly. “Or Hitler?” A redheaded adult heard and smiled and paused to watch. A lock of hair had fallen down the middle of the Butcher’s forehead, and as he sat Joggy like a charger, he did bear a faint resemblance to one of the grim little egomaniacs of the Dawn Era.
“Wrong millennium,” Hal said.
“Tamerlane then?” the Butcher pressed. “He killed cities and piled the skulls. Bloodbath stuff. Oh, yes, and Tamerlane was a Scand of the Navies.”
Hal looked puzzled and then quickly erased the expression. “Well, even if it is about Tamerlane, you can’t see it. How about it, Joggy?”
“They won’t let me in, either.”
“Yes, they will. You’re five years old now.”
“But I don’t feel any older,” Joggy replied doubtfully.
“The feeling comes at six. Don’t worry, the usher will notice the difference.”
Hal and Joggy switched off their levitators and dropped to their feet. The Butcher came down rather hard, twisting an ankle. He opened his mouth to cry, then abruptly closed it hard, bearing his pain in tight-lipped silence like an ancient soldier﻿—like Stalin, maybe, he thought. The redheaded adult’s face twitched in half-humorous sympathy.
Hal and Joggy mounted the Ramp and entered a twilit corridor which drank their faint footsteps and returned pulses of light. The Butcher limped manfully after them, but when he got inside, he forgot his battle injury.
---
Hal looked back. “Honestly, the usher will stop you.”
The Butcher shook his head. “I’m going to think my way in. I’m going to think old.”
“You won’t be able to fool the usher, Butcher. You under-fives simply aren’t allowed in the Time Theater.

===Chinese===

不过那总体感觉妙极了，悬浮器是最抢手的玩具之一。
“剧场到了。”乔吉宣布。
“*知道*。”屠夫没好气地说。
可连他的嗓音也带上了一点肃穆和收敛。从大坡道到最高处轻盈的尖顶，时间剧场是一场以超凡材质实现的神的梦。它给沿坡道上上下下的成年人罩上一层半神的光晕。
“我父亲还记得没有时间剧场的年月呢。”哈尔轻声说，一面扫视立面上一幅幅发光的图表和地图。“喏，正在看地球，公元前—公元时间轴零点前后，斯堪的纳维亚一带。应该有意思。”
“是讲拿破仑的吗？”屠夫急切地问。“还是希特勒？”一个红头发的成年人听见笑了，驻足看他。一塔头发垂在屠夫前额正中，他骑着乔吉如骑战马，还真有几分像曙光时代那班铁石心肠的小自我狂之一。
“千年差错了。”哈尔说。
“那是帖木儿？”屠夫追问。“他屠城堆骷髅。血浴场面的那种。哦对了，帖木儿还是个水师斯堪王。”
哈尔一脸茫然，随即赶紧抹掉这表情。“就算讲帖木儿，你也看不成。怎么样，乔吉？”
“他们也不让我进。”
“让的。你五岁了。”
“可我没觉得大了一岁。”乔吉将信将疑地答。
“六岁才有那感觉。别担心，引导场分辨得出来。”
哈尔和乔吉关掉悬浮器，落回脚上。屠夫下来得挺重，崴了脚脖子。他张嘴要哭，随即狠狠闭紧，闭着嘴默默忍痛，像个古代的士兵——没准像斯大林，他想。红发成年人的脸抽动了一下，带一半好笑的怜惜。
哈尔和乔吉登上坡道，走进一条幽暗的走廊，走廊吸走他们轻轻的脚步声，还回以一阵阵光的脉动。屠夫硬汉般一瘸一拐跟在后面，可一进里面，就忘了他的战伤。
---
哈尔回头。“说真的，引导场会拦住你的。”
屠夫摇头。“我要想进去。我要把自己想老。”
“你骗不过引导场的，屠夫。五岁以下就是不许进时间剧场。

===Original===

There’s a good reason for it﻿—something dangerous might happen if an under-five got inside.”
“Why?”
“I don’t exactly know, but something.”
“Hah! I bet they’re scared we’d go traveling in the Time Bubble and have some excitement.”
“They are not. I guess they just know you’d get bored and wander away from your seats and maybe disturb the adults or upset the electronics or something. But don’t worry about it, Butcher. The usher will take care of you.”
“Shut up﻿—I’m thinking I’m World Director,” the Butcher informed them, contorting his face diabolically.
Hal spoke to the uninjes, pointing to the side of the corridor. Obediently four of them lined up.
But Brute was peering down the corridor toward where it merged into a deeper darkness. His short legs stiffened, his neckless head seemed to retreat even further between his powerful shoulders, his lips writhed back to show his gleaming fangs, and a completely unfamiliar sound issued from his throat. A choked, grating sound. A growl. The other uninjes moved uneasily.
“Do you suppose something’s the matter with his circuits?” Joggy whispered. “Maybe he’s getting racial memories from the Scands.”
“Of course not,” Hal said irritably.
“Brute, get over there,” the Butcher commanded. Unwillingly, eyes still fixed on the blackness ahead, Brute obeyed.
The three boys started on. Hal and Joggy experienced a vaguely electrical tingling that vanished almost immediately. They looked back. The Butcher had been stopped by an invisible wall.
“I told you you couldn’t fool the usher,” Hal said.
The Butcher hurled himself forward. The wall gave a little, then bounced him back with equal force.
“I bet it’ll be a bum time view anyway,” the Butcher said, not giving up, but not trying again. “And I still don’t think the usher can tell how old you are. I bet there’s an overage teacher spying on you through a hole, and if he doesn’t like your looks, he switches on the usher.”
---
But the others had disappeared in the blackness. The Butcher waited and then sat down beside the uninjes. Brute laid his head on his knee and growled faintly down the corridor.
“Take it easy, Brute,” the Butcher consoled him. “I don’t think Tamerlane was really a Scand of the Navies anyhow.”
Two chattering girls hardly bigger than himself stepped through the usher as if it weren’t there.
The Butcher grimly slipped out the metal tube and put it to his lips.

这有充分的理由——五岁以下的进去，可能出危险的事。”
“为什么？”
“我说不清，反正有事。”
“哈！我打赌他们是怕我们钻进时间泡去旅行、找刺激。”

“闭嘴——我正在想我是世界指导者。”屠夫通知他们，脸拧成一副恶魔相。
哈尔对无伤犬们说话，指了指走廊边。四条乖乖排成一列。
可蛮牛正朝走廊深处凝望，望着它没入更黑的黑处。它的短腿绷直了，它那没脖子的脑袋仿佛在浑厚的肩膀之间缩得更深，嘴唇翻卷，露出雪亮的獠牙，喉咙里发出一种全然陌生的声音。一种憋住的、锉磨般的声音。一声咆哮。其他无伤犬不安地动起来。
“你说它的电路是不是出毛病了？”乔吉耳语。“没准它正从斯堪人那儿接收种族记忆呢。”
“当然不是。”哈尔烦躁地说。
“蛮牛，过去。”屠夫命令。蛮牛不情愿地、眼睛仍盯着前方黑处地照办了。
三个男孩继续走。哈尔和乔吉感到一阵微微的电麻，随即使刻消失。他们回头。屠夫被一堵看不见的墙拦住了。
“我说过你骗不过引导场。”哈尔说。
屠夫朝前猛撞。墙让了让，随即以同等的力道把他弹回来。
“我打赌反正也是场蹩脚的时间景。”屠夫说，不认输，也不再试。“我还是不信引导场分得出你几岁。我打赌有个超龄教师从洞里偷看你，他要看你不顺眼，就把引导场打开。”
---
可另外两人已消失在黑暗里。屠夫等了等，在无伤犬旁边坐下。蛮牛把头搁上他的膝盖，朝走廊低低咆哮。
“放轻松，蛮牛。”屠夫安慰它。“反正我也不信帖木儿真是水师斯堪王。”
两个叽叽喳喳、跟他差不多大的女孩径直穿过引导场，仿佛它不存在。
屠夫铁着脸抽出金属管，举到唇边。

===Original===

There were two closely spaced faint *plops* and a large green stain appeared on the bare back of one girl, while purple fluid dripped from the close-cropped hair of the other.
They glared at him and one of them said: “A cub!” But he had his arms folded and wasn’t looking at them.
Meanwhile, subordinate ushers had guided Hal and Joggy away from the main entrance to the Time Theater. A sphincter dilated and they found themselves in a small transparent cubicle from which they could watch the show without disturbing the adult audience. They unstrapped their levitators, laid them on the floor and sat down.
---
The darkened auditorium was circular. Rising from a low central platform was a huge bubble of light, its lower surface somewhat flattened. The audience was seated in concentric rows around the bubble, their keen and compassionate faces dimly revealed by the pale central glow.
But it was the scene within the bubble that riveted the attention of the boys.
Great brooding trees, the trunks of the nearer ones sliced by the bubble’s surface, formed the background. Through the dark, wet foliage appeared glimpses of a murky sky, while from the ceiling of the bubble, a ceaseless rain dripped mournfully. A hooded figure crouched beside a little fire partly shielded by a gnarled trunk. Squatting round about were wiry, blue-eyed men with shoulder-length blond hair and full blond beards. They were clothed in furs and metal-studded leather.
Here and there were scattered weapons and armor﻿—long swords glistening with oil to guard them from rust, crudely painted circular shields, and helmets from which curved the horns of beasts. Back and forth, lean, wolflike dogs paced with restless monotony.
---
Sometimes the men seemed to speak together, or one would rise to peer down the misty forest vistas, but mostly they were motionless. Only the hooded figure, which they seemed to regard with a mingled wonder and fear, swayed incessantly to the rhythm of some unheard chant.
“The Time Bubble has been brought to rest in one of the barbaric cultures of the Dawn Era,” a soft voice explained, so casually that Joggy looked around for the speaker, until Hal nudged him sharply, whispering with barely perceptible embarrassment: “Don’t do that, Joggy. It’s just the electronic interpreter. It senses our development and hears our questions and then it automats background and answers.

===Chinese===

两声轻微的、紧挨着的“噗噗”，一个女孩光裸的背上洇开一大团绿渍，另一个女孩剪短的头发上滴着紫水。
她们瞪他，其中一个说：“小崽子！”可他抱着胳膊，看也不看她们。
与此同时，次级引导场已把哈尔和乔吉从时间剧场正门引开。一道括约门张开，他们发现自己置身一间透明小包厢，可以从那儿看演出而不搅扰成年观众。他们解下悬浮器放在地上，坐下了。
---
暗下来的观众厅是圆的。从中央一座低台升起一个巨大的光泡，底面略平。观众围着光泡一圈圈同心排坐，机敏而悲悯的脸庞被中央的微光淡淡照出。
可吸住男孩们全部注意力的，是泡里的景象。
森森的大树构成背景，近处几棵的树干被泡面切去半边。潮湿的黑叶隙间露出阴沉的天一角，泡顶上，一场不停的雨凄凄地滴着。一个兜帽的身影蹲在一小堆火旁，火半掩在一根虬结的树干后。四下蹲坐着筋腱结实、蓝眼睛的男人，金发披肩，金须满面。他们穿兽皮和钉金属的皮甲。
四处散着兵刃甲胄——抹了油防锈的长剑、涂画粗陋的圆盾、弯出兽角的头盔。瘦长的狼样的狗来回踱着，单调不休。
---
有时男人们像在交谈，或有人起身朝雾蒙蒙的林间深处张望，但多数时候他们一动不动。只有那个兜帽身影——他们对它似乎又敬又惧——随着某种听不见的诵咒的节律不停摇晃。
“时间泡已停驻在曙光时代的一个蛮族文化中。”一个柔和的声音解说道，随意得让乔吉四下找说话的人，直到哈尔狠狠捅他一下，带着几乎察觉不出的窘迫耳语：“别这样，乔吉。那只是电子解说器。它感知我们的发育程度，听见我们的问题，然后自动调出背景并作答。

===Original===

But it’s no more alive than an adolescer or a kinderobot. Got a billion microtapes, though.”
The interpreter continued: “The skin-clad men we are viewing in Time in the Round seem to be a group of warriors of the sort who lived by pillage and rapine. The hooded figure is a most unusual find. We believe it to be that of a sorcerer who pretended to control the forces of nature and see into the future.”
Joggy whispered: “How is it that we can’t see the audience through the other side of the bubble? We can see through this side, all right.”
“The bubble only shines light out,” Hal told him hurriedly, to show he knew some things as well as the interpreter. “Nothing, not even light, can get into the bubble from outside. The audience on the other side of the bubble sees into it just as we do, only they’re seeing the other way﻿—for instance, they can’t see the fire because the tree is in the way. And instead of seeing us beyond, they see more trees and sky.”
Joggy nodded. “You mean that whatever way you look at the bubble, it’s a kind of hole through time?”
“That’s right.” Hal cleared his throat and recited: “The bubble is the locus of an infinite number of one-way holes, all centering around two points in space-time, one now and one then. The bubble looks completely open, but if you tried to step inside, you’d be stopped﻿—and so would an atom beam. It takes more energy than an atom beam just to maintain the bubble, let alone maneuver it.”
“I see, I guess,” Joggy whispered. “But if the hole works for light, why can’t the people inside the bubble step out of it into our world?”
“Why﻿—er﻿—you see, Joggy﻿—”
The interpreter took over. “The holes are one-way for light, but no-way for matter. If one of the individuals inside the bubble walked toward you, he would cross-section and disappear. But to the audience on the opposite side of the bubble, it would be obvious that he had walked away along the vista down which they are peering.”
---
As if to provide an example, a figure suddenly materialized on their side of the bubble. The wolflike dogs bared their fangs. For an instant, there was only an eerie, distorted, rapidly growing silhouette, changing from bloodred to black as the boundary of the bubble cross-sectioned the intruding figure.

可它不比辅导机或育儿机器人更有生命。倒是有十亿盘微型磁带。”
解说器继续道：“我们正在环景时间中观看的这些披皮之人，似乎是一伙靠劫掠为生的武士。那兜帽身影是一项极不寻常的发现。我们相信那是一名术士，自称能驾驭自然之力、洞见未来。”
乔吉耳语：“怎么泡的那一面我们看不透观众？这一面我们明明看得透。”
“泡只向外照光。”哈尔赶紧告诉他，以显示解说器会的他也懂些。“任何东西，连光也算上，都进不了泡。泡另一面的观众同我们一样看进泡里，只是方向相反——比如他们看不见火，因为树挡着。他们看到的不是我们这边，而是更多的树和天。”
乔吉点头。“你是说，不管从哪边看泡，它都是一种穿透时间的洞？”
“正是。”哈尔清清嗓子背诵道：“泡是无穷多个单向洞的轨迹，全部汇于时空中的两点，一点在今，一点在昔。泡看上去完全敞开，可你要想踏进去，就会被挡住——原子束也一样。光是维持这个泡，耗能就超过一支原子束，更别说操纵它了。”
“我懂了，大概。”乔吉耳语。“可既然洞对光管用，泡里的人为什么不能跨出洞到我们这个世界来？”
“这个——呃——你瞧，乔吉——”
解说器接了过去。“这些洞对光是单向的，对物质是无门。泡里的人若是朝你走来，他会被切成截面、消失不见。但对泡对面的观众来说，他显然只是顺着他们张望的那条深景走远了。”

像是要做个示范，一个身影突然在泡的这一面凝出来。狼样的狗龇出獠牙。有一瞬，那只是一个怪诞的、扭曲的、迅速长大的剪影，随着泡的边界切过闯入者，从血红变作黑。

Then they recognized the back of another long-haired warrior and realized that the audience on the other side of the bubble had probably seen him approaching for some time.

Hal turned and saw that two cold-eyed girls had been ushered into the cubicle. One was wiping her close-cropped hair with one hand while mopping a green stain from her friend’s back with the other.
Hal nudged Joggy and whispered: “Butch!”
But Joggy was still hypnotized by the Time Bubble.
“Then how is it, Hal,” he asked, “that light comes out of the bubble, if the people don’t? What I mean is, if one of the people walks toward us, he shrinks to a red blot and disappears. Why doesn’t the light coming our way disappear, too?”
“Well﻿—you see, Joggy, it isn’t real light. It’s﻿—”
Once more the interpreter helped him out.
“The light that comes from the bubble is an isotope. Like atoms of one element, photons of a single frequency also have isotopes. It’s more than a matter of polarization. One of these isotopes of light tends to leak futureward through holes in space-time. Most of the light goes down the vistas visible to the other side of the audience. But one isotope is diverted through the walls of the bubble into the Time Theater. Perhaps, because of the intense darkness of the theater, you haven’t realized how dimly lit the scene is. That’s because we’re getting only a single isotope of the original light. Incidentally, no isotopes have been discovered that leak pastward, though attempts are being made to synthesize them.”
“Oh, explanations!” murmured one of the newly arrived girls. “The cubs are always angling for them. Apple-polishers!”
“*I* like this show,” a familiar voice announced serenely. “They cut anybody yet with those choppers?”
Hal looked down beside him. “Butch! How did you manage to get in?”
“I don’t see any blood. Where’s the bodies?”
“But how *did* you get in﻿—Butcher?”

哈尔回头，看见两个冷眼的女孩被引进了包厢。一个正单手擦她剪短的头发，另一只手替同伴抹背上的绿渍。
哈尔捅捅乔吉，耳语：“布奇！”
可乔吉还被时间泡催眠着。
“那怎么会呢，哈尔，”他问，“人出不来，光却出得来？我是说，泡里的人朝我们走，就缩成一个红点消失了。朝我们来的光怎么不消失？”

The Butcher replied airily: “A redheaded man talked to me and said it certainly was sad for a future dictator not to be able to enjoy scenes of carnage in his youth, so I told him I’d been inside the Time Theater and just come out to get a drink of water and go to the eliminator, but then my sprained ankle had got worse﻿—I kind of tried to get up and fell down again﻿—so he picked me up and carried me right through the usher.”
“Butcher, that wasn’t honest,” Hal said a little worriedly. “You tricked him into thinking you were older and his brain waves blanketed yours, going through the usher. I really *have* heard it’s dangerous for you under-fives to be in here.”
“The way those cubs beg for babying and get it!” one of the girls commented. “Talk about sex favoritism!” She and her companion withdrew to the far end of the cubicle.
The Butcher grinned at them briefly and concentrated his attention on the scene in the Time Bubble.
“Those big dogs﻿—” he began suddenly. “Brute must have smelled ’em.”
“Don’t be silly,” Hal said. “Smells can’t come out of the Time Bubble. Smells haven’t any isotopes and﻿—”
“I don’t care,” the Butcher asserted. “I bet somebody’ll figure out someday how to use the bubble for time traveling.”
“You can’t travel in a point of view,” Hal contradicted, “and that’s all the bubble is. Besides, some scientists think the bubble isn’t real at all, but a﻿—uh﻿—”
“I believe,” the interpreter cut in smoothly, “that you’re thinking of the theory that the Time Bubble operates by hypermemory. Some scientists would have us believe that all memory is time traveling and that the basic location of the bubble is not space-time at all, but ever-present eternity. Some of them go so far as to state that it is only a mental inability that prevents the Time Bubble from being used for time traveling﻿—just as it may be a similar disability that keeps a robot with the same or even more scopeful memories from being a real man or animal.
“It is because of this minority theory that underage individuals and other beings with impulsive mentalities are barred from the Time Theater. But do not be alarmed. Even if the minority theory should prove true﻿—and no evidence for it has ever appeared﻿—there are automatically operating safeguards to protect the audience from any harmful consequences of time traveling (almost certainly impossible, remember) in either direction.”

“屠夫，那可不诚实。”哈尔有点担忧地说。“你骗他信了你比实际大，过引导场时他的脑波罩住了你的。我真的*听说*过五岁以下的进来危险。”

屠夫朝她们咧嘴一笑，随即把注意力集中到时间泡的景象上。

“你没法在一个视点里旅行，”哈尔反驳，“而泡的全部就是一个视点。再说，有些科学家认为泡根本不是真的，而是——呃——”
“我想，”解说器从容接口，“你想到的是时间泡以超记忆运作的理论。有些科学家要我们相信，一切记忆都是时间旅行，泡的根本位置根本不在时空里，而在无时不在的永恒中。他们中有人甚至说，只是一种心智上的无能，才使时间泡不能用于时间旅行——正如一种类似的障碍，使一台记忆容量相同乃至更大的机器人（robot）成不了真正的人或动物。
“正因为这一少数派理论，未成年者和其它心智冲动的生灵才被拒于时间剧场之外。但不必惊慌。即使少数派理论被证明为真——而支持它的证据从未出现过——也有自动运作的保险装置，保护观众免受朝任一方向时间旅行（记住，几乎可以肯定不可能）的一切有害后果。”

“Sissies!” was the Butcher’s comment.
---
“You’re rather young to be here, aren’t you?” the interpreter inquired.
The Butcher folded his arms and scowled.
The interpreter hesitated almost humanly, probably snatching through a quarter-million microtapes. “Well, you wouldn’t have got in unless a qualified adult had certified you as plus-age. Enjoy yourself.”
There was no need for the last injunction. The scene within the bubble had acquired a gripping interest. The shaggy warriors were taking up their swords, gathering about the hooded sorcerer. The hood fell back, revealing a face with hawklike, disturbing eyes that seemed to be looking straight out of the bubble at the future.
“This is getting good,” the Butcher said, squirming toward the edge of his seat.
“Stop being an impulsive mentality,” Hal warned him a little nervously.
“Hah!”
The sorcerer emptied the small bag on the fire and a thick cloud of smoke puffed toward the ceiling of the bubble. A clawlike hand waved wildly. The sorcerer appeared to be expostulating, commanding. The warriors stared uncomprehendingly, which seemed to exasperate the sorcerer.
“That’s right,” the Butcher approved loudly. “Sock it to ’em!”
“Butcher!” Hal admonished.
Suddenly the bubble grew very bright, as if the Sun had just shone forth in the ancient world, though the rain still dripped down.
“A viewing anomaly has occurred,” the interpreter announced. “It may be necessary to collapse the Time Bubble for a short period.”
In a frenzy, his ragged robes twisting like smoke, the sorcerer rushed at one of the warriors, pushing him backward so that in a moment he must cross-section.
“Attaboy!” the Butcher encouraged.
Then the warrior was standing outside the bubble, blinking toward the shadows, rain dripping from his beard and furs.
“Oh, *boy*!” the Butcher cheered in ecstasy.
“Butcher, you’ve done it!” Hal said, aghast.
“I sure did,” the Butcher agreed blandly, “but that old guy in the bubble helped me. Must take two to work it.”
“Keep your seats!” the interpreter said loudly. “We are energizing the safeguards!”
---
The warriors inside the bubble stared in stupid astonishment after the one who had disappeared from their view. The sorcerer leaped about, pushing them in his direction.
Abrupt light flooded the Time Theater. The warriors who had emerged from the bubble stiffened themselves, baring their teeth.

===Original===

“The safeguards are now energized,” the interpreter said.
A woman in a short golden tunic stood up uncertainly from the front row of the audience.
The first warrior looked her up and down, took one hesitant step forward, then another, then suddenly grabbed her and flung her over his left shoulder, looking around menacingly and swinging his sword in his right hand.
“I repeat, the safeguards have been fully energized! Keep your seats!” the interpreter enjoined.
In the cubicle, Hal and Joggy gasped, the two girls squeaked, but the Butcher yelled a “Hey!” of disapproval, snatched up something from the floor and darted out through the sphincter.
Here and there in the audience, other adults stood up. The emerged warriors formed a ring of swinging swords and questing eyes. Between their legs their wolfish dogs, emerged with them, crouched and snarled. Then the warriors began to fan out.
“There has been an unavoidable delay in energizing the safeguards,” the interpreter said. “Please be patient.”
At that moment, the Butcher entered the main auditorium, brandishing a levitator above his head and striding purposefully down the aisle. At his heels, five stocky forms trotted. In a definitely pre-civilization voice, or at least with pre-civilization volume, he bellowed: “Hey, you! You quit that!”
The first warrior looked toward him, gave his left shoulder a shake to quiet his wriggling captive, gave his right shoulder one to supple his sword arm, and waited until the dwarfish challenger came into range. Then his sword swished down in a flashing arc.
Next moment, the Butcher was on his knees and the warrior was staring at him open-mouthed. The sword had rebounded from something invisible an arm’s length above the gnomelike creature’s head. The warrior backed a step.
---
The Butcher stayed down, crouching half behind an aisle seat and digging for something in his pocket. But he didn’t stay quiet. “Sic ’em, Brute!” he shrilled. “Sic ’em, Darter! Sic ’em, Pinkie and Whitie and Blue!” Then he stopped shouting and raised his hand to his mouth.
---
Growling quite unmechanically, the five uninjes hurled themselves forward and closed with the warrior’s wolflike dogs. At the first encounter, Brute and Pinkie were grabbed by the throats, shaken, and tossed a dozen feet. The warriors snarled approval and advanced.

“保险装置充能出现不可避免的延迟。”解说器说。“请耐心。”
就在这时，屠夫进了主观众厅，悬浮器举在头上挥舞，目标明确地大步跨过走道。他脚后小跑着五个矮壮的身影。他用一种绝对文明之前的嗓门——至少是文明之前的音量——吼道：“嘿，说你呢！住手！”
头一名武士朝他望来，耸耸左肩镇住扭动的俘虏，又耸耸右肩活络剑臂，然后等着这小矮个挑战者进入范围。他的剑呼地劈下一道亮弧。
下一刻，屠夫跪在地上，武士目瞪口呆地瞪着他。剑从那侏儒头顶一臂远处什么看不见的东西上弹开了。武士倒退一步。
---
屠夫伏得低低的，半躲在一把走道座椅后面，在口袋里掏什么。可他嘴上没停。“上，蛮牛！”他尖声喊。“上，飞镖！上，粉粉、白白、蓝蓝！”喊完他停了停，把手举到嘴边。
---
五条无伤犬发出全无机械感的咆哮，猛扑上去，同武士们的狼狗咬作一团。头一个照面，蛮牛和粉粉就被咬住喉咙、甩得团团转、抛出十几英尺。武士们狞笑着叫好，逼上前来。

===Original===

But then Brute and Pinkie raced back eagerly to the fight﻿—and suddenly the face of the leading warrior was drenched with scarlet. He blinked and touched his fingers to it, then looked at his hand in horror.
The Butcher spared a second to repeat his command to the uninjes. But already the battle was going against the larger dogs. The latter had the advantage of weight and could toss the smaller dogs like so many foxes. But their terrible fangs did no damage, and whenever an uninj clamped on a throat, that throat was torn out.
Meanwhile, great bloody stains had appeared on the bodies of all the warriors. They drew back in a knot, looking at each other fearfully. That was when the Butcher got to his feet and strode forward, hand clenching the levitator above his head.
“Get back where you belong, you big jerks! And drop that lady!”
The first warrior pointed toward him and hissed something. Immediately, a half dozen swords were smiting at the Butcher.
“We are working to energize the safeguards,” the interpreter said in mechanical panic. “Remain patient and in your seats.”
The uninjes leaped into the melee, at first tearing more fur than flesh. Swords caught them and sent them spinning through the air. They came yapping back for more. Brute fixed on the first warrior’s ankle. He dropped the woman, stamped unavailingly on the uninj, and let out a screech.
Swords were still rebounding from the invisible shield under which the Butcher crouched, making terrible faces at his attackers. They drew back, looked again at their bloodstains, goggled at the demon dogs. At their leader’s screech, they broke and plunged back into the Time Bubble, their leader stumbling limpingly after them. There they wasted no time on their own ragged sorcerer. Their swords rose and fell, and no repulsor field stayed them.
“Brute, come back!” the Butcher yelled.
---
The gray uninj let go his hold on the leader’s ankle and scampered out of the Time Bubble, which swiftly dimmed to its original light intensity and then winked out.
For once in their very mature lives, all of the adults in the auditorium began to jabber at each other simultaneously.
“We are sorry, but the anomaly has made it necessary to collapse the Time Bubble,” the interpreter said. “There will be no viewing until further announcement. Thank you for your patience.”

===Chinese===

可随即蛮牛和粉粉又热切地扑回战团——眨眼间，领头武士的脸上浇满了猩红。他眨眨眼，手指一抹，随即惊恐地看着自己的手。
屠夫匀出一秒重申对无伤犬的号令。但战局早已倒向大狗。大狗占着体重的便宜，把小狗一条条像狐狸似的抛起来。可它们的獠牙伤不了敌手分毫，而无伤犬一旦咬住哪条喉咙，那喉咙就被生生撕开。
与此同时，所有武士身上都现出大块大块血渍。他们抱成一团退缩，惊惧地互相看。就在这时屠夫站起身，大步上前，手把悬浮器攥在头顶。
“滚回你们该在的地方去，你们这帮大蠢货！放下那位女士！”
头一名武士朝他一指，嘶声说了句什么。立时六柄剑朝屠夫劈下。
“我们正在全力充能保险装置。”解说器以机械的恐慌说。“请保持耐心，坐好。”
无伤犬们跃入混战，起初撕下的毛多过肉。剑锋碰上它们，把它们抽打得满天飞旋。它们又狺狺叫着扑回来。蛮牛咬定头一名武士的脚踝。他撇下女人，徒劳地跺那无伤犬，发出一声长嚎。
剑还在从屠夫头顶看不见的护罩上弹开，屠夫蹲在罩下，朝攻击者做着狰狞的鬼脸。他们退开，再看看自己身上的血渍，又目瞪口呆地看看这群恶魔般的狗。听到首领长嚎，他们溃散了，一头扎回时间泡里，首领一瘸一拐跟在后面。进了泡，他们对自家那个衣衫褴褛的术士毫不客气。剑起剑落，再没有排斥场拦得住。
“蛮牛，回来！”屠夫大喊。
---
灰色无伤犬松开首领的脚踝，蹦出时间泡。泡迅速暗回原先的亮度，随即熄灭了。
这些极成熟的成年人平生头一回，满场同时朝彼此叽叽喳喳起来。
“很抱歉，异常迫使我们必须收拢时间泡。”解说器说。“另行通知前暂停观看。谢谢各位的耐心。”

===Original===

Hal and Joggy caught up with the Butcher just as Brute jumped into his arms and the woman in gold picked him up and hugged him fiercely. The Butcher started to pull away, then grudgingly submitted.
“Cubs!” came a small cold voice from behind Hal and Joggy. “Always playing hero! Say, what’s that awful smell, Cynthia? It must have come from those dirty past men.”
Hal and Joggy were shouting at the Butcher, but he wasn’t listening to them or to the older voices clamoring about “revised theories of reality” and other important things. He didn’t even squirm as Brute licked his cheek and the woman in gold planted a big kiss practically on his mouth.
He smiled dreamily and stroked Brute’s muzzle and murmured softly: “We came, we saw, we conquered, didn’t we, Brute?”

===Chinese===

哈尔和乔吉追上屠夫时，蛮牛正跳进他怀里，金衣女人把他抱起来狠狠搂住。屠夫挣了一下，随即不情愿地受了。
“小崽子！”哈尔和乔吉身后传来一个细而冷的声音。“永远在扮英雄！嗬，这是什么怪味，辛西娅？准是那些脏兮兮的旧时代男人身上沾来的。”
哈尔和乔吉朝屠夫嚷嚷，可他既没听他们，也没听那些为“修正的现实理论”和别的要紧事吵成一团的成年嗓音。蛮牛舐他的脸颊、金衣女人几乎亲在他嘴上的一大口，他连扭都没扭一下。
他梦一般地笑着，抚着蛮牛的口鼻，轻声呢喃：“我们来了，我们看见了，我们征服了，是不是，蛮牛？”

## What’s He Doing in There? / 他在里面干什么？
===Original===

The Professor was congratulating Earth’s first visitor from another planet on his wisdom in getting in touch with a cultural anthropologist before contacting any other scientists (or governments, God forbid!), and in learning English from radio and TV before landing from his orbit-parked rocket, when the Martian stood up and said hesitantly, “Excuse me, please, but where is it?”
That baffled the Professor and the Martian seemed to grow anxious﻿—at least his long mouth curved upward, and he had earlier explained that it curling downward was his smile﻿—and he repeated, “Please, where is it?”
He was surprisingly humanoid in most respects, but his complexion was textured so like the rich dark armchair he’d just been occupying that the Professor’s pinstriped gray suit, which he had eagerly consented to wear, seemed an arbitrary interruption between him and the chair﻿—a sort of Mother Hubbard dress on a phantom conjured from its leather.
The Professor’s Wife, always a perceptive hostess, came to her husband’s rescue by saying with equal rapidity, “Top of the stairs, end of the hall, last door.”
The Martian’s mouth curled happily downward and he said, “Thank you very much,” and was off.
Comprehension burst on the Professor. He caught up with his guest at the foot of the stairs.
“Here, I’ll show you the way,” he said.
“No, I can find it myself, thank you,” the Martian assured him.
---
Something rather final in the Martian’s tone made the Professor desist, and after watching his visitor sway up the stairs with an almost hypnotic softly jogging movement, he rejoined his wife in the study, saying wonderingly, “Who’d have thought it, by George! Function taboos as strict as our own!”
“I’m glad some of your professional visitors maintain ’em,” his wife said darkly.
“But this one’s from Mars, darling, and to find out he’s﻿—well, similar in an aspect of his life is as thrilling as the discovery that water is burned hydrogen. When I think of the day not far distant when I’ll put his entries in the cross-cultural index﻿ ﻿…”
He was still rhapsodizing when the Professor’s Little Son raced in.
“Pop, the Martian’s gone to the bathroom!”
“Hush, dear. Manners.”
“Now it’s perfectly natural, darling, that the boy should notice and be excited. Yes, Son, the Martian’s not so very different from us.”
“Oh, certainly,” the Professor’s Wife said with a trace of bitterness.

===Chinese===

教授正在祝贺地球的第一位外星来客——恭喜他明智：在联络其他科学家（更别说各国政府了，老天保佑别！）之前先找文化人类学家接洽，又先从广播电视学英语再从停泊轨道上的火箭降落——这时火星人站起身，迟疑地说：“对不起，请问它在哪儿？”
这话把教授问住了，火星人显得焦急起来——至少他的长嘴向上弯了，而他早先解释过，向下卷才是他的微笑——他重复道：“请问，它在哪儿？”
他在大多数方面出人意料地像人，只是他的肤色质感太像他刚坐过的那把深色扶手椅，以至于他急切同意穿上的教授那套细条纹灰西装，看起来像他与椅子之间一个武断的打断——像给一个从皮革里召来的幽灵套上的宽大罩衫。
教授太太素来是位善解人意的主人，以同样的语速救了丈夫的驾：“楼梯顶上，走廊尽头，最后一扇门。”
火星人的嘴愉快地向下卷了卷，说：“多谢，”便去了。
教授恍然大悟。他在楼梯脚下追上客人。
“来，我给你带路，”他说。
“不用，我自己找得到，谢谢你，”火星人向他保证。
---
火星人话音里有种相当决绝的东西，教授便作罢了。目送客人以一种几乎催眠的、轻柔颠动的步态摇摆上楼之后，他回到书房与妻子会合，惊叹道：“谁想得到，老天！功能禁忌跟我们的一样严格！”
“我很高兴你那些职业访客里还有人守着它，”他太太阴阴地说。
“可这一位是火星来的呀，亲爱的，发现他在生活的某一面——嗯——与我们相似，就像发现水是烧过的氢一样激动人心。一想到不久的将来我要把他的条目编入跨文化索引……”
他还沉浸在狂想中，教授的小儿子飞奔进来。
“爸，火星人上厕所了！”
“嘘，宝贝。要有礼貌。”
“孩子注意到并且兴奋，这完全自然，亲爱的。是的，儿子，火星人跟我们没有什么天差地别。”
“哦，可不是嘛，”教授太太说，带一丝苦味。

===Original===

“I don’t imagine his turquoise complexion will cause any comment at all when you bring him to a faculty reception. They’ll just figure he’s had a hard night﻿—and that he got that baby-elephant nose sniffing around for assistant professorships.”
“Really, darling! He probably thinks of our noses as disagreeably amputated and paralyzed.”
“Well, anyway, Pop, he’s in the bathroom. I followed him when he squiggled upstairs.”
“Now, Son, you shouldn’t have done that. He’s on a strange planet and it might make him nervous if he thought he was being spied on. We must show him every courtesy. By George, I can’t wait to discuss these things with Ackerly-Ramsbottom! When I think of how much more this encounter has to give the anthropologist than even the physicist or astronomer﻿ ﻿…”
He was still going strong on his second rhapsody when he was interrupted by another high-speed entrance. It was the Professor’s Coltish Daughter.
“Mom, Pop, the Martian’s﻿—”
“Hush, dear. We know.”
The Professor’s Coltish Daughter regained her adolescent poise, which was considerable. “Well, he’s still in there,” she said. “I just tried the door and it was locked.”
“I’m glad it was!” the Professor said while his wife added, “Yes, you can’t be sure what﻿—” and caught herself. “Really, dear, that was very bad manners.”
“I thought he’d come downstairs long ago,” her daughter explained. “He’s been in there an awfully long time. It must have been a half hour ago that I saw him gyre and gimbal upstairs in that real gone way he has, with Nosy here following him.” The Professor’s Coltish Daughter was currently soaking up both jive and *Alice*.
---
When the Professor checked his wristwatch, his expression grew troubled. “By George, he is taking his time! Though, of course, we don’t know how much time Martians﻿ ﻿… I wonder.”
“I listened for a while, Pop,” his son volunteered. “He was running the water a lot.”
“Running the water, eh? We know Mars is a water-starved planet. I suppose that in the presence of unlimited water, he might be seized by a kind of madness and﻿ ﻿… But he seemed so well adjusted.”
Then his wife spoke, voicing all their thoughts. Her outlook on life gave her a naturally sepulchral voice.
“*What’s he doing in there?*”
Twenty minutes and at least as many fantastic suggestions later, the Professor glanced again at his watch and nerved himself for action.

===Chinese===

“我不认为他那一身绿松石色皮肤会在你带他出席教职员招待会时引起任何议论。人家只会以为他熬夜熬狠了——还会以为他那只小象鼻子是到处嗅副教授职位嗅出来的。”
“真的，亲爱的！他多半还觉得我们的鼻子难看地被截肢、瘫痪了呢。”
“反正，爸，他在浴室里。他扭上楼的时候我跟去了。”
“听我说，儿子，你不该那样。他身在陌生的星球，要是觉得被监视，可能会紧张的。我们必须对他礼数周全。老天，我等不及跟阿克利-拉姆斯博顿讨论这些了！一想到这次相遇能给人类学家的东西，连物理学家或天文学家都比不上……”
他的第二波狂想正澎湃着，又一次高速闯入打断了他。是教授那半大不小的女儿。
“妈，爸，火星人——”
“嘘，宝贝。我们知道。”
教授的半大女儿找回了她那相当可观的青春期矜持。“嗯，他还在里面，”她说。“我刚试了门，锁着呢。”
“锁着才好！”教授说，他太太跟着补一句：“是啊，你没法确定会不会——”随即打住。“说真的，宝贝，那非常失礼。”
“我以为他早下来了，”女儿解释。“他在里面待了好久好久。我看见他打着他那套真够呛的转体上楼，鼻子探路，后面还跟着小跟班，准有半个钟头了。”教授的半大女儿眼下正同时猛补黑话和《爱丽丝》。
---
教授看了看腕表，神情忧虑起来。“老天，他可真从容！当然，我们不知道火星人的时间……我纳闷。”
“我听了一阵，爸，”儿子自告奋勇。“他老在放水。”
“放水，嗯？我们知道火星是缺水的行星。我猜想，面对无限的水，他也许会被一种疯狂攫住，从而……可他看上去那么适应良好。”
随后他太太开口，道出了所有人的心思。她的人生观赋予她天然的坟腔嗓音。
“他在里面干什么？”
二十分钟和至少同样多的奇思妙想之后，教授又看了一眼表，鼓起行动的勇气。

===Original===

Motioning his family aside, he mounted the stairs and tiptoed down the hall.
He paused only once to shake his head and mutter under his breath, “By George, I wish I had Fenchurch or von Gottschalk here. They’re a shade better than I am on intercultural contracts, especially taboo-breakings and affronts﻿ ﻿…”
His family followed him at a short distance.
The Professor stopped in front of the bathroom door. Everything was quiet as death.
He listened for a minute and then rapped measuredly, steadying his hand by clutching its wrist with the other. There was a faint splashing, but no other sound.
Another minute passed. The Professor rapped again. Now there was no response at all. He very gingerly tried the knob. The door was still locked.
When they had retreated to the stairs, it was the Professor’s Wife who once more voiced their thoughts. This time her voice carried overtones of supernatural horror.
“*What’s he doing in there?*”
“He may be dead or dying,” the Professor’s Coltish Daughter suggested briskly. “Maybe we ought to call the Fire Department, like they did for old Mrs. Frisbee.”
The Professor winced. “I’m afraid you haven’t visualized the complications, dear,” he said gently. “No one but ourselves knows that the Martian is on Earth, or has even the slightest inkling that interplanetary travel has been achieved. Whatever we do, it will have to be on our own. But to break in on a creature engaged in﻿—well, we don’t know what primal private activity﻿—is against all anthropological practice. Still﻿—”
“Dying’s a primal activity,” his daughter said crisply.
“So’s ritual bathing before mass murder,” his wife added.
“Please! Still, as I was about to say, we do have the moral duty to succor him if, as you all too reasonably suggest, he has been incapacitated by a germ or virus or, more likely, by some simple environmental factor such as Earth’s greater gravity.”
“Tell you what, Pop﻿—I can look in the bathroom window and see what he’s doing. All I have to do is crawl out my bedroom window and along the gutter a little ways. It’s safe as houses.”
---
The Professor’s question beginning with, “Son, how do you know﻿—” died unuttered and he refused to notice the words his daughter was voicing silently at her brother.

===Chinese===

他示意家人让开，登上楼梯，蹑手蹑脚走过走廊。
他只停了一次，摇头低语：“老天，我真希望芬彻奇或冯·戈特沙尔克在这儿。跨文化契约方面他们比我强一丝，尤其是破忌与冒犯……”
家人隔一小段距离跟着他。
教授在浴室门前站定。四下死寂。
他听了一分钟，然后有节制地叩门，一只手抓住另一只手的手腕来稳住它。里面传来轻微的溅水声，再无别的动静。
又过了一分钟。教授再叩。这下全无回应。他极小心地试旋门把。门还锁着。
退到楼梯口后，又是教授太太道出众人心声。这一回她的嗓音里带着超自然的恐怖。
“他在里面干什么？”
“他可能正在死或垂死，”教授的半大女儿干脆地建议。“也许我们该叫消防队，像他们给老弗里斯比太太做的那样。”
教授缩了一下。“恐怕你没有想清楚其中的复杂，宝贝，”他温和地说。“除了我们，没人知道火星人在地球上，甚至没人稍稍察觉星际旅行已经实现。不管做什么，我们只能靠自己。可闯进去打扰一个正从事——嗯，从事什么原始私秘活动的生物，违背一切人类学惯例。不过——”
“垂死就是原始活动，”女儿干脆地说。
“大屠杀前的仪式沐浴也是，”他太太补充。
“拜托！不过，正像我要说的，假如——正如你们全都有理有据地设想的那样——他被某种细菌或病毒、或者更可能被某个简单的环境因素比如地球较强的重力弄垮了，我们确实有道义责任去救助他。”
“爸，这样——我可以从浴室窗户往里看，看他到底在干什么。我只要从我的卧室窗户爬出去，沿檐沟挪一小段。安全得很。”
---
教授那句以“儿子，你怎么知道——”开头的问题没出口，他也假装没看见女儿正冲她弟弟无声翕动的嘴唇。

===Original===

He glanced at his wife’s sardonically composed face, thought once more of the Fire Department and of other and larger and even more jealous﻿—or would it be skeptical?﻿—government agencies, and clutched at the straw offered him.
Ten minutes later, he was quite unnecessarily assisting his son back through the bedroom window.
“Gee, Pop, I couldn’t see a sign of him. That’s why I took so long. Hey, Pop, don’t look so scared. He’s in there, sure enough. It’s just that the bathtub’s under the window and you have to get real close up to see into it.”
“The Martian’s taking a bath?”
“Yep. Got it full up and just the end of his little old schnozzle sticking out. Your suit, Pop, was hanging on the door.”
The one word the Professor’s Wife spoke was like a death knell.
“*Drowned!*”
“No, Ma, I don’t think so. His schnozzle was opening and closing regular like.”
“Maybe he’s a shape-changer,” the Professor’s Coltish Daughter said in a burst of evil fantasy. “Maybe he softens in water and thins out after a while until he’s like an eel and then he’ll go exploring through the sewer pipes. Wouldn’t it be funny if he went under the street and knocked on the stopper from underneath and crawled into the bathtub with President Rexford, or Mrs. President Rexford, or maybe right into the middle of one of Janey Rexford’s Oh-I’m-so-sexy bubble baths?”
“Please!” The Professor put his hand to his eyebrows and kept it there, cuddling the elbow in his other hand.
“Well, have you thought of something?” the Professor’s Wife asked him after a bit. “What are you going to do?”
The Professor dropped his hand and blinked his eyes hard and took a deep breath.
“Telegraph Fenchurch and Ackerly-Ramsbottom and then break in,” he said in a resigned voice, into which, nevertheless, a note of hope seemed also to have come. “First, however, I’m going to wait until morning.”
And he sat down cross-legged in the hall a few yards from the bathroom door and folded his arms.
---
So the long vigil commenced.
The Professor’s family shared it and he offered no objection. Other and sterner men, he told himself, might claim to be able successfully to order their children to go to bed when there was a Martian locked in the bathroom, but he would like to see them faced with the situation.
Finally dawn began to seep from the bedrooms. When the bulb in the hall had grown quite dim, the Professor unfolded his arms.

===Chinese===

他瞥了一眼太太讥诮而镇定的脸，又想了想消防队，以及其他更大、更好妒忌——还是说多疑？——的政府机构，一把抓住了递到面前的稻草。
十分钟后，他全然多余地扶着儿子从卧室窗户爬回来。
“哎呀，爸，我一点他的影子也没看见。所以才去了那么久。嘿，爸，别吓成这样。他确实在里面，错不了。只是浴缸在窗户底下，你得凑得特近才看得见里面。”
“火星人在洗澡？”
“嗯。缸放得满满的，就他那只小老鼻子尖露在外面。你的西装，爸，挂在门上。”
教授太太说出的一个字，像丧钟。
“淹死了！”
“没，妈，我不觉得。他的鼻子尖挺有规律地一开一合。”
“没准他是个变形者，”教授的半大女儿一阵恶毒的幻想发作。“没准他在水里泡软了，过一阵就摊细成一条鳗鱼，然后他就会顺着下水管道去探险。要是他从街底下钻过去，从底下敲那个塞子，爬进雷克斯福德总统的浴缸——或者雷克斯福德总统夫人——或者一头扎进珍妮·雷克斯福德那场‘哦人家好性感’的泡泡浴中间，那多逗啊？”
“拜托！”教授把手按上眉毛，按在那里不动，另一只手抱着这手的肘。
“嗯，你想到什么办法了吗？”过了一会儿教授太太问他。“你打算怎么办？”
教授放下手，使劲眨眼，深吸一口气。
“给芬彻奇和阿克利-拉姆斯博顿发电报，然后破门，”他用听天由命的声音说，不过那声音里似乎也掺进了一丝希望。“但首先，我要等到天亮。”
他在离浴室门几码的走廊上盘腿坐下，抱起双臂。
---
漫长的守夜开始了。
教授的家人分享着它，他也不反对。别的一些更严厉的男人，他对自己说，或许会声称能成功地命令孩子们在浴室里锁着一个火星人的时候上床睡觉，但他倒想看看他们亲自面对这种局面。
终于，黎明开始从几间卧室渗出来。走廊的灯泡变得相当昏暗时，教授展开双臂。

===Original===

Just then, there was a loud splashing in the bathroom. The Professor’s family looked toward the door. The splashing stopped and they heard the Martian moving around. Then the door opened and the Martian appeared in the Professor’s gray pinstripe suit. His mouth curled sharply downward in a broad alien smile as he saw the Professor.
“Good morning!” the Martian said happily. “I never slept better in my life, even in my own little wet bed back on Mars.”
He looked around more closely and his mouth straightened. “But where did you all sleep?” he asked. “Don’t tell me you stayed dry all night! You *didn’t* give up your only bed to me?”
His mouth curled upward in misery. “Oh, dear,” he said, “I’m afraid I’ve made a mistake somehow. Yet I don’t understand how. Before I studied you, I didn’t know what your sleeping habits would be, but that question was answered for me﻿—in fact, it looked so reassuringly homelike﻿—when I saw those brief TV scenes of your females ready for sleep in their little tubs. Of course, on Mars, only the fortunate can always be sure of sleeping wet, but here, with your abundance of water, I thought there would be wet beds for all.”
He paused. “It’s true I had some doubts last night, wondering if I’d used the right words and all, but then when you rapped ‘Good night’ to me, I splashed the sentiment back at you and went to sleep in a wink. But I’m afraid that somewhere I’ve blundered and﻿—”
“No, no, dear chap,” the Professor managed to say. He had been waving his hand in a gentle circle for some time in token that he wanted to interrupt. “Everything is quite all right. It’s true we stayed up all night, but please consider that as a watch﻿—an honor guard, by George!﻿—which we kept to indicate our esteem.”

===Chinese===

正在那时，浴室里响起哗啦一声水响。教授一家望向门。水声停了，他们听见火星人走动。然后门开了，火星人穿着教授的细条纹灰西装出现。看见教授，他的嘴猛地向下一弯，咧出一个宽阔的外星微笑。
“早上好！”火星人愉快地说。“我这辈子从没睡得这么好过，连在火星我自己那张小湿床上也没有。”
他更仔细地环顾四周，嘴直了起来。“可你们都在哪儿睡的？”他问。“你们该不会整夜都保持干燥吧！你们该不会把唯一的床让给了我？”
他的嘴痛苦地向上弯。“哦，天哪，”他说，“恐怕我在哪儿弄出了差错。可我不明白怎么会。研究你们之前，我不知道你们的睡眠习惯会是什么样，可那个问题替我解答了——事实上它看上去那么让人放心地亲切——当我看见电视里那些你们女性准备就寝的短镜头，她们躺在各自的小浴缸里。当然，在火星上，只有幸运儿才能永远确保睡得湿，可在这儿，凭着你们丰沛的水，我以为人人都有湿床。”
他顿了顿。“确实，昨晚我有过些疑虑，怀疑我用词对不对，可后来你们向我敲‘晚安’，我就把这份情意泼回给你们，一眨眼就睡着了。可我恐怕我在哪一步犯了错，而且——”
“不，不，亲爱的伙计，”教授总算说出话来。他的手已经在一小圈一小圈温和地挥了一阵子，示意他想插话。“一切都好得很。我们确实彻夜未眠，但请把它当作一次守夜——一支仪仗，老天！——我们以此来表达敬意。”

## X Marks the Pedwalk / 人行横道上的叉
===Original===

> Based in material in Ch. 7﻿—“First Clashes of the Wheeled and Footed Sects”﻿—of Vol. 3 of Burger’s monumental *History of Traffic*, published by the Foundation for Twenty-Second Century Studies.

===Chinese===

> 本文取材于二十二世紀研究基金会出版的伯格鸿篇巨制《交通史》第三卷第七章——“轮派与足派的最初冲突”。

===Original===

The raggedy little old lady with the big shopping bag was in the exact center of the crosswalk when she became aware of the big black car bearing down on her.
Behind the thick bulletproof glass its seven occupants had a misty look, like men in a diving bell.
She saw there was no longer time to beat the car to either curb. Veering remorselessly, it would catch her in the gutter.
Useless to attempt a feint and double-back, such as any venturesome child executed a dozen times a day. Her reflexes were too slow.
Polite vacuous laughter came from the car’s loudspeaker over the engine’s mounting roar.
From her fellow pedestrians lining the curbs came a sigh of horror.
The little old lady dipped into her shopping bag and came up with a big blue-black automatic. She held it in both fists, riding the recoils like a rodeo cowboy on a bucking bronco.
Aiming at the base of the windshield, just as a big-game hunter aims at the vulnerable spine of a charging water buffalo over the horny armor of its lowered head, the little old lady squeezed off three shots before the car chewed her down.
From the right-hand curb a young woman in a wheelchair shrieked an obscenity at the car’s occupants.
Smythe-de Winter, the driver, wasn’t happy. The little old lady’s last shot had taken two members of his car pool. Bursting through the laminated glass, the steel-jacketed slug had traversed the neck of Phipps-McHeath and buried itself in the skull of Horvendile-Harker.
Braking viciously, Smythe-de Winter rammed the car over the right-hand curb. Pedestrians scattered into entries and narrow arcades, among them a youth bounding high on crutches.
But Smythe-de Winter got the girl in the wheelchair.
Then he drove rapidly out of the Slum Ring into the Suburbs, a shred of rattan swinging from the flange of his right fore mudguard for a trophy. Despite the two-for-two casualty list, he felt angry and depressed. The secure, predictable world around him seemed to be crumbling.
---
While his companions softly keened a dirge to Horvy and Phipps and quietly mopped up their blood, he frowned and shook his head.
“They oughtn’t to let old ladies carry magnums,” he murmured.
Witherspoon-Hobbs nodded agreement across the front-seat corpse. “They oughtn’t to let ’em carry anything. God, how I hate Feet,” he muttered, looking down at his shrunken legs. “Wheels forever!” he softly cheered.

===Chinese===

那个拎着大购物袋、衣衫褴褛的小老太太，走到人行横道正中央时，才察觉那辆黑色大车正朝她压过来。
厚厚的防弹玻璃后，车里七个人影朦朦胧胧，像潜水钟里的汉子。
她看出已经来不及抢在车前赶到任何一边的路缘了。它毫不留情地一偏，将在阴沟里逮住她。
想虚晃一枪再折回来也是白搭——那是任何胆大的孩子一天都要玩上十来次的花招。她的反应太慢了。
车顶扩音器里传出彬彬有礼却空洞的笑声，压过引擎渐高的咆哮。
排列在两侧路缘的步行者们发出一声恐怖的叹息。
小老太太把手探进购物袋，掏出一支蓝黑色的大号自动手枪。她双手攥着它，像竞技牛仔骑在暴跳的野马上那样驾驭着后坐力。
她瞄着挡风玻璃的根部——正如大型猎物猎人瞄着冲锋的水牛那低下头角的角质盔甲之下脆弱的脊柱——在汽车把她碾倒之前，小老太太打出了三发。
右侧路缘，一个坐轮椅的年轻女人冲车里的人尖声咒骂了一句脏话。
司机史迈斯-德温特（Smythe-de Winter）不痛快。小老太太的最后一发带走了他拼车组的两名成员。钢壳子弹穿透夹层玻璃，贯过菲普斯-麦克希思的脖子，埋进霍文迪尔-哈克的头骨。
史迈斯-德温特狠狠刹车，把车撞过右侧路缘。步行者四散逃进门洞和窄廊，其中有一个拄拐腾跃得很高的小伙子。
可史迈斯-德温特逮住了轮椅姑娘。
随后他疾驶出贫民环带，开进郊区，右前挡泥板翻边上挂着一小条藤椅篾，权当战利品。尽管伤亡二比二，他又怒又丧。他身边这个安稳、可预测的世界似乎正在崩塌。
---
同伴们为霍维和菲普斯低声唱着挽歌、静静擦拭血迹时，他皱着眉摇头。
“他们不该让老太太们带大口径枪，”他咕哝。
威瑟斯庞-霍布斯（Witherspoon-Hobbs）隔着前座上的尸体点头同意。“他们该什么都不让她们带。上帝，我恨透了‘脚’，”他低头看着自己萎缩的双腿咕哝。“轮子万岁！”他轻声欢呼。

===Original===

The incident had immediate repercussions throughout the city. At the combined wake of the little old lady and the girl in the wheelchair, a fiery-tongued speaker inveighed against the White-Walled Fascists of Suburbia, telling to his hearers, the fabled wonders of old Los Angeles, where pedestrians were sacrosanct, even outside crosswalks. He called for a hobnail march across the nearest lawn-bowling alleys and perambulator-traversed golf courses of the motorists.
At the Sunnyside Crematorium, to which the bodies of Phipps and Horvy had been conveyed, an equally impassioned and rather more grammatical orator reminded his listeners of the legendary justice of old Chicago, where pedestrians were forbidden to carry small arms and anyone with one foot off the sidewalk was fair prey. He broadly hinted that a holocaust, primed if necessary with a few tankfuls of gasoline, was the only cure for the Slums.
Bands of skinny youths came loping at dusk out of the Slum Ring into the innermost sections of the larger doughnut of the Suburbs slashing defenseless tires, shooting expensive watchdogs and scrawling filthy words on the pristine panels of matrons’ runabouts which never ventured more than six blocks from home.
Simultaneously squadrons of young suburban motorcycles and scooterites roared through the outermost precincts of the Slum Ring, harrying children off sidewalks, tossing stink-bombs through second-story tenement windows and defacing hovel-fronts with sprays of black paint.
Incident﻿—a thrown brick, a cut corner, monster tacks in the portico of the Auto Club﻿—were even reported from the center of the city, traditionally neutral territory.
The Government hurriedly acted, suspending all traffic between the Center and the Suburbs and establishing a 24-hour curfew in the Slum Ring. Government agents moved only by centipede-car and pogo-hopper to underline the point that they favored neither contending side.
The day of enforced non-movement for Feet and Wheels was spent in furtive vengeful preparations. Behind locked garage doors, machine-guns that fired through the nose ornament were mounted under hoods, illegal scythe blades were welded to oversize hubcaps and the stainless steel edges of flange fenders were honed to razor sharpness.

===Chinese===

事件的反响立刻传遍全城。在小老太太和轮椅姑娘的合并守灵会上，一位烈火之舌的演讲者痛斥郊区的白圈法西斯，向听众们讲述老洛杉矶的传说奇观——那里步行者神圣不可侵犯，连人行横道之外也是。他号召来一场钉靴进军，踏遍驾车人最近的草坪保龄球场和婴儿车出没的高尔夫球场。
在接纳了菲普斯和霍维遗体的向阳火葬场，一位同样激昂、文法却讲究些的演说家向听众忆起老芝加哥的传奇司法——那里步行者禁止携带轻武器，任何人只要一脚离开人行道便是合法猎物。他露骨地暗示，一场大清洗——必要时用几箱汽油引燃——是贫民窟唯一的疗法。
黄昏时分，一伙伙瘦削的少年从贫民环带一路小跑进郊区大甜甜圈最内圈的各区，砍防御无门的轮胎，射昂贵的看门狗，还在太太们那从不离家六条街的敞篷小车的崭新车板上涂写下流字眼。
与此同时，一队队郊区的年轻摩托和踏板车手轰鸣着穿过贫民环带最外围的街区，把孩子们从人行道上撵走，往二层廉租公寓的窗户里扔臭弹，用黑漆喷涂精蹋棚屋门脸。
连城市中心——传统上的中立领土——也报出了事端：一块飞砖，一次抄近角，汽车俱乐部门廊上一排巨大的图钉。
政府仓促行动，中止了中心与郊区之间的一切交通，并在贫民环带实行二十四小时宵禁。政府特工只乘蜈蚣车和跳跃杆出行，以强调他们不偏袒任何争斗一方。
对“脚”与“轮”双方，这个强制停动的日子都在鬼祟而复仇性的备战中度过。锁闭的车库门后，机枪被装上引擎盖，可以从车头饰标处射击；非法的镰刀刀片焊上加大轮毂盖；挡泥板翻边的不锈钢边缘磨出了剃刀的锋利。

===Original===

While nervous National Guardsmen hopped about the deserted sidewalks of the Slum Ring, grim-faced men and women wearing black armbands moved through the webwork of secret tunnels and hidden doors, distributing heavy-caliber small arms and spike-studded paving blocks, piling cobblestones on strategic rooftops and sapping upward from the secret tunnels to create car-traps. Children got ready to soap intersections after dark. The Committee of Pedestrian Safety, sometimes known as Robespierre’s Rats, prepared to release its two carefully hoarded antitank guns.
---
At nightfall, under the tireless urging of the Government, representatives of the Pedestrians and the Motorists met on a huge safety island at the boundary of the Slum Ring and the Suburbs.
Underlings began a noisy dispute as to whether Smythe-de Winter had failed to give a courtesy honk before charging, whether the little old lady had opened fire before the car had come within honking distance, how many wheels of Smythe-de’s car had been on the sidewalk when he hit the girl in the wheelchair and so on. After a little while the High Pedestrian and the Chief Motorist exchanged cautious winks and drew aside.
The red writhing of a hundred kerosene flares and the mystic yellow pulsing of a thousand firefly lamps mounted on yellow sawhorses ranged around the safety island illumined two tragic, strained faces.
“A word before we get down to business,” the Chief Motorist whispered. “What’s the current S.Q. of your adults?”
“Forty-one and dropping,” the High Pedestrian replied, his eyes fearfully searching from side to side for eavesdroppers. “I can hardly get aides who are halfway compos mentis.”
“Our own Sanity Quotient is thirty-seven,” the Chief Motorist revealed. He shrugged helplessly﻿ ﻿… “The wheels inside my people’s heads are slowing down. I do not think they will be speeded up in my lifetime.”
“They say Government’s only fifty-two,” the other said with a matching shrug.
“Well, I suppose we must scrape out one more compromise,” the one suggested hollowly, “though I must confess there are times when I think we’re all the figments of a paranoid’s dream.”
Two hours of concentrated deliberations produced the new Wheel-Foot Articles of Agreement. Among other points, pedestrian handguns were limited to a slightly lower muzzle velocity and to .

===Chinese===

紧张的国民警卫队员在贫民环带闃无人迹的人行道上蹦跳巡逻时，戴黑纱的冷酷男女们穿行在暗道与秘门的蛛网中，分发大口径轻武器和钉刺遍布的铺路块，把鹅卵石堆上战略性屋顶，并从暗道向上掘进，制造陷车陷阱。孩子们准备天黑后往路口打肥皂。行人安全委员会——有时被称为罗伯斯庇尔鼠帮——准备放出他们苦心囤积的两门反坦克炮。
---
入夜，在政府不倦的敦促下，步行者与驾车人的代表们在贫民环带与郊区交界处一座巨大的安全岛上会面。
下属们吵吵嚷嚷地争起来：史迈斯-德温特冲锋前有没有按礼节鸣一声笛；小老太太开火时车是否已进入鸣笛距离；史迈斯-德撞上轮椅姑娘时，车有几个轮子压在人行道上，等等。过了一会儿，步行魁首与驾车首脑谨慎地交换了一个眼色，退到一旁。
一百支煤油火把的红光扭动，绕着安全岛的一千盏萤火灯安装在黄色锯木马上发出神秘的黄色脉动，照亮两张悲剧而紧绷的脸。
“谈正事前先问一句，”驾车首脑低声说。“你们成年人的最新 S.Q. 是多少？”
“四十一，还在跌，”步行魁首答，双眼惶惶地左右提防偷听者。“我几乎找不到脑子还算一半清醒的帮手。”
“我们自己的清醒商数是三十七，”驾车首脑透露。他无助地耸耸肩……“我的人民脑袋里的轮子正在慢下来。我看我这辈子是快不起来了。”
“听说政府的才五十二，”对方回以同样的一耸。
“唉，我想我们只好再勉强拼凑一个妥协，”这位空空洞洞地说，“虽然我得承认，有时候我觉得我们统统是某个偏执狂梦里的虚构。”
两小时专注的审议产出了新的轮足协议条款。其中要点有：步行者手枪被限制为略低的枪口初速和点三八口径以下，而驾车人冲撞人行横道上的步行者前，须在一街区距离外鸣笛三声。两个轮子压过路缘，把交通杀人从三级过失杀人降格为轻罪杀人。盲人步行者获准携带手榴弹。

===Original===

38 caliber and under, while motorists were required to give three honks at one block distance before charging a pedestrian in a crosswalk. Two wheels over the curb changed a traffic kill from third-degree manslaughter to petty homicide. Blind pedestrians were permitted to carry hand grenades.
Immediately the Government went to work. The new Wheel-Foot Articles were loudspeakered and posted. Detachments of police and psychiatric social hoppers centipedaled and pogoed through the Slum Ring, seizing outsize weapons and giving tranquilizing jet-injections to the unruly. Teams of hypnotherapists and mechanics scuttled from home to home in the Suburbs and from garage to garage, in-chanting a conformist serenity and stripping illegal armament from cars. On the advice of a rogue psychiatrist, who said it would channel off aggressions, a display of bullfighting was announced, but this had to be canceled when a strong protest was lodged by the Decency League, which had a large mixed Wheel-Foot membership.
At dawn, curfew was lifted in the Slum Ring and traffic reopened between the Suburbs and the Center. After a few uneasy moments it became apparent that the status quo had been restored.
---
Smythe-de Winter tooled his gleaming black machine along the Ring. A thick steel bolt with a large steel washer on either side neatly filled the hole the little old lady’s slug had made in the windshield.
A brick bounced off the roof. Bullets pattered against the side windows.
Smythe-de ran a handkerchief around his neck under his collar and smiled.
A block ahead children were darting into the street, catcalling and thumbing their noses. Behind one of them limped a fat dog with a spiked collar.
Smythe-de suddenly gunned his motor. He didn’t hit any of the children, but he got the dog.
A flashing light on the dash showed him the right front tire was losing pressure. Must have hit the collar as well! He thumbed the matching emergency-air button and the flashing stopped.
He turned toward Witherspoon-Hobbs and said with thoughtful satisfaction, “I like a normal orderly world, where you always have a little success, but not champagne-heady; a little failure, but just enough to brace you.”
Witherspoon-Hobbs was squinting at the next crosswalk. Its center was discolored by a brownish stain ribbon-tracked by tires.
“That’s where you bagged the little old lady, Smythe-de,” he remarked.

===Chinese===

政府立刻动手。新的轮足协议条款用喇叭广播、四处张贴。警察与精神科社工小队乘着蜈蚣车和跳跃杆穿行贫民环带，收缴超规格武器，给不驯者注射镇定喷剂。催眠治疗师和机械师小组在郊区挨家挨户、逐库逐库地窜，吟诵着顺从的宁静，从汽车上拆下非法武装。一位离经叛道的精神病学家建议举办一场斗牛表演，说这能疏导攻击性，但体面联盟提出强烈抗议后只得取消——该联盟有大批轮足混合成员。
黎明，贫民环带解除宵禁，郊区与中心之间的交通重开。片刻的不安之后，显然，现状已然恢复。
---
史迈斯-德温特驾着他那辆锃亮的黑色机器沿环带走。一支粗钢螺栓，两边各垫一个大钢垫圈，恰好堵住小老太太的子弹在挡风玻璃上打的洞。
一块砖从车顶弹开。子弹噼噼啪啪打在侧窗上。
史迈斯-德拿手帕在领子底下绕脖子抹了一圈，微笑。
前方一个街区，孩子们正窜进街道，吹口哨嘲骂，做捏鼻子的鬼脸。其中一个身后跛行着一条戴钉圈的肥狗。
史迈斯-德突然踩下油门。他一个孩子也没撞着，可他撞到了狗。
仪表盘上一盏灯闪示右前胎正在失压。准是也蹭到那个钉圈了！他按下配套的应急充气钮，闪烁停了。
他转向威瑟斯庞-霍布斯，带着深思的满足说：“我喜欢正常有序的世界，你总能有一点点成功，但不至于香槟上头；有一点点失败，但刚好够你振作。”
威瑟斯庞-霍布斯正眯眼望向下一个人行横道。横道中央被一道褐色的、被轮胎描出带状轨迹的污渍弄脏了。
“你收掉那个小老太太的地方，史迈斯-德，”他说。

===Original===

“I’ll say this for her now: she had spirit.”
“Yes, that’s where I bagged her,” Smythe-de agreed flatly. He remembered wistfully the witchlike face growing rapidly larger, her jerking shoulders in black bombazine, the wild white-circled eyes. He suddenly found himself feeling that this was a very dull day.

===Chinese===

“我现在得替她说一句：她有精气神。”
“对，那是我收掉她的地方，”史迈斯-德平平地表示同意。他怅然想起那张巫婆似的脸飞快放大，她黑绸衣里耸动的双肩，那双野性的、围着白圈的眼睛。他忽然觉得，这一天非常乏味。

### I / 一
===Original===

The narrow cove was quiet as the face of an expectant child, yet so near the ruffled Atlantic that the last push of wind carried the *Annie O.* its full length. The man in gray flannels and sweatshirt let the sail come crumpling down and hurried past its white folds at a gait made comically awkward by his cramped muscles. Slowly the rocky ledge came nearer. Slowly the blue V inscribed on the cove’s surface by the sloop’s prow died. Sloop and ledge kissed so gently that he hardly had to reach out his hand.
He scrambled ashore, dipping a sneaker in the icy water, and threw the line around a boulder. Unkinking himself, he looked back through the cove’s high and rocky mouth at the gray-green scattering of islands and the faint dark line that was the coast of Maine. He almost laughed in satisfaction at having disregarded vague warnings and done the thing every man yearns to do once in his lifetime﻿—gone to the farthest island out.
He must have looked longer than he realized, because by the time he dropped his gaze the cove was again as glassy as if the *Annie O.* had always been there. And the splotches made by his sneaker on the rock had faded in the hot sun. There was something very unusual about the quietness of this place. As if time, elsewhere hurrying frantically, paused here to rest. As if all changes were erased on this one bit of Earth.
The man’s lean, melancholy face crinkled into a grin at the banal fancy. He turned his back on his new friend, the little green sloop, without one thought for his nets and specimen bottles, and set out to explore. The ground rose steeply at first and the oaks were close, but after a little way things went downhill and the leaves thinned and he came out on more rocks﻿—and realized that he hadn’t quite gone to the farthest one out.
---
Joined to this island by a rocky spine, which at the present low tide would have been dry but for the spray, was another green, high island that the first had masked from him all the while he had been sailing. He felt a thrill of discovery, just as he’d wondered back in the woods whether his might not be the first human feet to kick through the underbrush. After all, there were thousands of these islands.
Then he was dropping down the rocks, his lanky limbs now moving smoothly enough.
To the landward side of the spine, the water was fairly still.

===Chinese===

狭窄的小湾安静得像一张期待的孩子脸，却又离波涛起伏的大西洋那么近，最后一股风把《安妮·奥号》一路送了进来。穿灰法兰烯裤和运动衫的男人放帆让它皱着落下，匆匆从它的白色褶皱旁跑过，僵硬的肌肉让步态滑稽地笨拙。礁石脊缓缓靠近。单桅帆船船首在海湾水面划出的蓝色 V 字缓缓消逝。帆船与礁石吻得那么轻，他几乎不必伸手。
他爬上岸，一只运动鞋浸了冰水，把缆绳绕上一块圆石。他伸展着身子，回望海湾那高而多岩的口外，散落着的灰绿色岛屿，以及缅因海岸那道模糊的暗线。他几乎要满意地笑出声——他无视了含糊的警告，做成了每个男人一辈子都想做一次的事：去到最远的那座岛。
他一定望了比自知更久，因为等他垂下目光，海湾已重新平静如镜，仿佛《安妮·奥号》从来就在那里。他的运动鞋在岩石上留下的斑点，也在烈日下褪去了。这地方的安静有些非常不寻常。仿佛时间——在别处疯狂赶路的时间——在此地停下来歇脚。仿佛一切变化在这小小一角地球上都被抹去。
男人清瘦、忧郁的脸皱成一个咧嘴笑，笑这平庸的遐想。他把背转向他的新朋友、那艘绿色小单桅船，全不想他的网子和标本瓶，出发去探察。地面起初陡升，橡树密匝，可走了一小段便向下走，树叶稀疏了，他又走上更多的岩石——并意识到，自己并没有去到最远的那座。
---
与他这座岛以一条岩石脊椎相连——眼下低潮时若非浪花飞溅本可干走过去——的是另一座绿色高岛，此前他一路扬帆时，头一座岛一直把它遮着。他感到一阵发现的战栗，正如刚才在林子里他曾暗想，踢开灌木的会不会是第一双人的脚。毕竟，这样的岛有成千上万。
随后他顺岩石而下，瘦长的四肢此刻已动作顺滑。
在脊椎的向陆一侧，水相当静。

===Original===

It even began with another deep cove, in which he glimpsed the spiny spheres of sea urchins. But from seaward the waves chopped in, sprinkling his trousers to the knees and making him wince pleasurably at the thought of what vast wings of spray and towers of solid water must crash up from here in a storm.
He crossed the rocks at a trot, ran up a short grassy slope, raced through a fringe of trees﻿—and came straight up against an eight-foot fence of heavy mesh topped with barbed wire and backed at a short distance with high, heavy shrubbery.
Without pausing for surprise﻿—in fact, in his holiday mood, using surprise as a goad﻿—he jumped for the branch of an oak whose trunk touched the fence, scorning the easier lower branch on the other side of the tree. Then he drew himself up, worked his way to some higher branches that crossed the fence, and dropped down inside.
Suddenly cautious, he gently parted the shrubbery and, before the first surprise could really sink in, had another.
---
A closely mown lawn dotted with more shrubbery ran up to a snug white Cape Cod cottage. The single strand of a radio aerial stretched the length of the roof. Parked on a neat gravel driveway that crossed just in front of the cottage was a short, square-lined touring car that he recognized from remembered pictures as an ancient Essex. The whole scene had about it the same odd quietness as the cove.
Then, with the air of a clockwork toy coming to life, the white door opened and an elderly woman came out, dressed in a long, lace-edged dress and wide, lacy hat. She climbed into the driver’s seat of the Essex, sitting there very stiff and tall. The motor began to chug bravely, gravel skittered, and the car rolled off between the trees.
The door of the house opened again and a slim girl emerged. She wore a white silk dress that fell straight from square neckline to hip-height waistline, making the skirt seem very short. Her dark hair was bound with a white bandeau so that it curved close to her cheeks. A dark necklace dangled against the white of the dress. A newspaper was tucked under her arm.
She crossed the driveway and tossed the paper down on a rattan table between three rattan chairs and stood watching a squirrel zigzag across the lawn.
---
The man stepped through the wall of shrubbery, called, “hello!” and walked toward her.

===Chinese===

它甚至以又一个深湾开始，湾里他瞥见海胆的刺球。可从向海一侧，碎浪劈进来，浪花洒湿他到膝盖，让他愉快地一缩——想到风暴时该有多大的浪花之翼、水的高塔会从这里轰然砸起。
他小跑着越过岩石，跑上一道短短的草坡，冲过一带树缘——一头撞上一道八英尺的重型铁丝围栏，顶上带刺丝，隔一小段距离后又有一道高大茂密的灌木。
他不停下来惊讶——事实上，趁着假日心气，他把惊讶当马刺——他纵身抓住一根橡树枝，树干挨着围栏，他偏看不上树另一侧那根更容易够到的低枝。随后他引体向上，挪到几根跨过围栏的高枝上，翻落进去。
突然谨慎起来，他轻轻分开灌木，还没等第一个惊讶真正沉下去，又来了一个。
---
一片修剪得很短的草坪，缀着更多灌木，向上延到一座 snug 的白色科德角式小屋。单股无线电天线沿屋脊拉满。停在屋前横过的整洁砾石车道上的，是一辆方正短小的旅行车，他凭记忆中的图片认出是一辆老式埃塞克斯。整个场景带着与小湾同样的古怪宁静。
随后，像发条玩具活了过来，白门打开，一位年长的妇人出来，穿一条镶蕾丝的长裙，戴一顶宽大的蕾丝帽。她爬进埃塞克斯的驾驶座，坐得笔挺。马达勇敢地突突起来，砾石迸溅，汽车在树间驶远。
屋门再开，一个苗条的姑娘出来。她穿一条白丝绸裙，从方领直落到齐腰的腰线，衬得裙子很短。她的黑发用一条白束发带绾着，紧贴双颊弯出弧线。一条深色项链垂在裙子的白上。腋下夹着一份报纸。
她穿过车道，把报纸丢在三把藤椅中间的藤桌上，站着看一只松鼠 Z 字形窜过草坪。
---
男人穿过灌木之墙，喊了一声“你好！”朝她走去。

===Original===

She whirled around and stared at him as still as if her heart had stopped beating. Then she darted behind the table and waited for him there. Granting the surprise of his appearance, her alarm seemed not so much excessive as eerie. As if, the man thought, he were not an ordinary stranger, but a visitor from another planet.
Approaching closer, he saw that she was trembling and that her breath was coming in rapid, irregular gasps. Yet the slim, sweet, patrician face that stared into his had an underlying expression of expectancy that reminded him of the cove. She couldn’t have been more than eighteen.
He stopped short of the table. Before he could speak, she stammered out, “Are you he?”
“What do you mean?” he asked, smiling puzzledly.
“The one who sends me the little boxes.”
“I was out sailing and I happened to land in the far cove. I didn’t dream that anyone lived on this island, or even came here.”
“No one ever does come here,” she replied. Her manner had changed, becoming at once more wary and less agitated, though still eerily curious.
“It startled me tremendously to find this place,” he blundered on. “Especially the road and the car. Why, this island can’t be more than a quarter of a mile wide.”
“The road goes down to the wharf,” she explained, “and up to the top of the island, where my aunts have a tree-house.”
He tore his mind away from the picture of a woman dressed like Queen Mary clambering up a tree. “Was that your aunt I saw driving off?”
“One of them. The other’s taken the motorboat in for supplies.” She looked at him doubtfully. “I’m not sure they’ll like it if they find someone here.”
“There are just the three of you?” he cut in quickly, looking down the empty road that vanished among the oaks.
She nodded.
“I suppose you go in to the mainland with your aunts quite often?”
She shook her head.
“It must get pretty dull for you.”
“Not very,” she said, smiling. “My aunts bring me the papers and other things. Even movies. We’ve got a projector. My favorite stars are Antonio Morino and Alice Terry. I like her better even than Clara Bow.”
He looked at her hard for a moment. “I suppose you read a lot?”
She nodded. “Fitzgerald’s my favorite author.” She started around the table, hesitated, suddenly grew shy. “Would you like some lemonade?”
---
He’d noticed the dewed silver pitcher, but only now realized his thirst.

===Chinese===

她猛地转身，凝视他，静得仿佛心脏停止了跳动。随后她闪到桌子后面，在那里等他过来。就算他的出现够惊人，她的惊惶也谈不上过度，而是诡异。仿佛，男人想，他不是个普通的陌生人，而是另一个行星来的访客。
走近些，他看见她在发抖，呼吸急促而紊乱。可那张盯着他的、苗条甜美、贵妇式的脸，底子里带着一种期待的神情，让他想起那个小湾。她不会超过十八岁。
他在桌前停住。还没等他开口，她结结巴巴地说：“是你吗？”
“你指什么？”他问，困惑地微笑。
“给我送小盒子的那个人。”
“我出来驾船，碰巧在远处的海湾靠了岸。我做梦也没想到这岛上住着人，甚至有人来。”
“从来没有人来，”她答。她的态度变了，一下子更戒备也更平静了，却依然诡异地好奇。
“找到这地方让我大大吃了一惊，”他笨嘴拙舌地继续。“尤其是路和汽车。嗐，这岛宽不过四分之一英里。”
“路通到下面的码头，”她解释，“也通到岛顶，我的姨母们在上面有一座树屋。”
他把一个像玛丽王后打扮的女人爬树的画面从脑子里赶走。“我刚看见开车走的是你姨母？”
“是其中一位。另一位开摩托艇去采买补给去了。”她狐疑地看他。“我不敢说她们发现有人在这儿会高兴。”
“就你们三个？”他赶快插话，望着那条消失在橡树间的空路。
她点头。
“我猜你常跟姨母们去大陆吧？”
她摇头。
“那你一定闷得慌。”
“不怎么闷，”她微笑着说。“姨母们给我带来报纸和其他东西。甚至电影。我们有一台放映机。我最喜欢的明星是安东尼奥·莫里诺和爱丽丝·特里。我喜欢她甚至超过克拉拉·鲍。”
他盯了她一会儿。“我猜你读很多书？”
她点头。“菲茨杰拉德是我最喜欢的作家。”她绕过桌子起步，又迟疑，突然害羞了。“想喝点柠檬水吗？”
---
他早注意到那只挂着水珠的银壶，可直到现在才意识到自己的渴。

===Original===

Yet when she handed him a glass, he held it untasted and said awkwardly, “I haven’t introduced myself. I’m Jack Barry.”
She stared at his outstretched right hand, slowly extended her own toward it, shook it up and down exactly once, then quickly dropped it.
He chuckled and gulped some lemonade. “I’m a biology student. Been working at Wood’s Hole the first part of the summer. But now I’m here to do research in marine ecology﻿—that’s sort of sea-life patterns﻿—of the inshore islands. Under the direction of Professor Kesserich. You know about him, of course?”
She shook her head.
“Probably the greatest living biologist,” he was proud to inform her. “Human physiology as well. Tremendous geneticist. In a class with Carlson and Jacques Loeb. Martin Kesserich﻿—he lives over there at town. I’m staying with him. You ought to have heard of him.” He grinned. “Matter of fact, I’d never have met you if it hadn’t been for Mrs. Kesserich.”
The girl looked puzzled.
Jack explained, “The old boy’s been off to Europe on some conferences, won’t be back for a couple days more. But I was to get started anyhow. When I went out this morning Mrs. Kesserich﻿—she’s a drab sort of person﻿—said to me, ‘Don’t try to sail to the farther islands.’ So, of course, I had to. By the way, you still haven’t told me your name.”
“Mary Alice Pope,” she said, speaking slowly and with an odd wonder, as if she were saying it for the first time.
“You’re pretty shy, aren’t you?”
“How would I know?”
The question stopped Jack. He couldn’t think of anything to say to this strangely attractive girl dressed almost like a “flapper.”
“Will you sit down?” she asked him gravely.
The rattan chair sighed under his weight. He made another effort to talk. “I’ll bet you’ll be glad when summer’s over.”
“Why?”
“So you’ll be able to go back to the mainland.”
“But I never go to the mainland.”
“You mean you stay out here all winter?” he asked incredulously, his mind filled with a vision of snow and frozen spray and great gray waves.
“Oh, yes. We get all our supplies on hand before winter. My aunts are very capable. They don’t always wear long lace dresses. And now I help them.”
“But that’s impossible!” he said with sudden sympathetic anger. “You can’t be shut off this way from people your own age!”
“You’re the first one I ever met.” She hesitated. “I never saw a boy or a man before, except in movies.”
“You’re joking!”

===Chinese===

可当她递给他一杯，他拿着没喝，笨拙地说：“我还没自我介绍。我叫杰克·巴里。”
她盯着他伸出的右手，慢慢把自己的手伸向它，精确地上下一握，随即飞快抽回。
他轻笑一声，灌下一口柠檬水。“我是生物学学生。夏天前半段在伍兹霍尔工作。现在来研究近岸岛屿的海洋生态学——就是海洋生物分布模式之类——由凯瑟里奇教授指导。你当然知道他吧？”
她摇头。
“大概是在世最伟大的生物学家，”他骄傲地告诉她。“人体生理学也精通。了不起的遗传学家。跟卡尔森和雅克·洛布一个级别。马丁·凯瑟里奇——他就住在那边镇上。我住他家。你该听说过他。”他咧嘴一笑。“说起来，要不是凯瑟里奇太太，我还碰不上你呢。”
姑娘露出困惑。
杰克解释：“老头子去欧洲开什么会了，还有几天才回来。但我反正得先干起来。今早我出门时，凯瑟里奇太太——她是个乏味的人——对我说：‘别想驾船去更远的岛。’于是，当然，我非去不可。对了，你还没告诉我你的名字。”
“玛丽·爱丽丝·波普，”她说，说得缓慢而带着一种奇异的惊叹，仿佛头一回说它。
“你挺害羞的，是不是？”
“我怎么会知道？”
这个问题把杰克问住了。他对这个穿得几乎像“飞来波女郎”的奇特迷人的姑娘，想不出话来说。
“坐一下吧？”她庄重地问他。
藤椅在他的重量下叹了口气。他再作一次谈话的努力。“我打赌，夏天结束时你会很高兴。”
“为什么？”
“这样你就能回大陆了呀。”
“可我从来不去大陆。”
“你是说你整个冬天都待在这儿？”他难以置信地问，脑中涌进雪、冻结的浪花和巨大的灰色波涛的图景。
“哦，是的。入冬前我们把全部补给备齐在手。我的姨母们非常能干。她们并不总穿镶蕾丝的长裙。现在我也帮她们。”
“可这不可能！”他带着突如其来的同情的怒气说。“他们不能这样把你和同龄人隔开！”
“你是我见到的第一个。”她迟疑。“我从没见过男孩或男人，除了电影里。”
“你开玩笑！”

===Original===

“No, it’s true.”
“But why are they doing it to you?” he demanded, leaning forward. “Why are they inflicting this loneliness on you, Mary?”
---
She seemed to have gained poise from his loss of it. “I don’t know why. I’m to find out soon. But actually I’m not lonely. May I tell you a secret?” She touched his hand, this time with only the faintest trembling. “Every night the loneliness gathers in around me﻿—you’re right about that. But then every morning new life comes to me in a little box.”
“What’s that?” he said sharply.
“Sometimes there’s a poem in the box, sometimes a book, or pictures, or flowers, or a ring, but always a note. Next to the notes I like the poems best. My favorite is the one by Matthew Arnold that ends,

> ‘Ah, love, let us be true

To one another! for the world, which seems

To lie before us like a land of dreams,

So various, so beautiful, so new,

Hath really neither joy, nor love, nor light,

Nor certitude﻿—’ ”

===Chinese===

“不，是真的。”
“可他们为什么这样对你？”他探身追问。“为什么把这种孤独强加给你，玛丽？”
---
她似乎从他的失态中赢得了镇定。“我不知道为什么。我不久就会知道。可其实我并不孤独。我能告诉你一个秘密吗？”她碰碰他的手，这一次只有最轻微的颤抖。“每天夜里，孤独从四面向我围拢——这一点你说对了。可每天早晨，新的生命装在一只小盒子里来到我这儿。”
“这是什么意思？”他尖锐地说。
“盒子里有时是一首诗，有时是一本书，或者图片，或者花，或者一枚戒指，但总有一张字条。字条之外我最喜欢诗。我最喜欢的是马修·阿诺德那首，结尾是：

> “啊，爱人，让我们彼此

> 忠诚！因为这个世界，看似

> 横陈在我们面前如一片梦土，

> 如此多彩，如此美丽，如此新奇，

> 实则既无欢乐，也无爱，也无光明，

> 也无确信——”

===Original===

“Wait a minute,” he interrupted. “Who sends you these boxes?”
“I don’t know.”
“But how are the notes signed?”
“They’re wonderful notes,” she said. “So wise, so gay, so tender, you’d imagine them being written by John Barrymore or Lindbergh.”
“Yes, but how are they signed?”
She hesitated. “Never anything but ‘Your Lover.’ ”
“And so when you first saw me, you thought﻿—” He began, then stopped because she was blushing.
“How long have you been getting them?”
“Ever since I can remember. I have two closets of the boxes. The new ones are either by my bed when I wake or at my place at breakfast.”
“But how does this﻿—person get these boxes to you out here? Does he give them to your aunts and do they put them there?”
“I’m not sure.”
“But how can they get them in winter?”
“I don’t know.”
“Look here,” he said, pouring himself more lemonade, “how long is it since you’ve been to the mainland?”
“Almost eighteen years. My aunts tell me I was born there in the middle of the war.”
“What war?” he asked startledly, spilling some lemonade.
“The World War, of course. What’s the matter?”
Jack Barr was staring down at the spilled lemonade and feeling a kind of terror he’d never experienced in his waking life. Nothing around him had changed. He could still feel the same hot sun on his shoulders, the same icy glass in his hand, scent the same lemon-acid odor in his nostrils. He could still hear the faint *chop-chop* of the waves.
And yet everything had changed, gone dark and dizzy as a landscape glimpsed just before a faint. All the little false notes had come to a sudden focus. For the lemonade had spilled on the headline of the newspaper the girl had tossed down, and the headline read:

Under the big black banner of that head swam smaller ones:

===Chinese===

“等一下，”他打断。“这些盒子是谁寄给你的？”
“我不知道。”
“可那些字条怎么署名？”
“那些字条妙极了，”她说。“那么睿智，那么快活，那么温柔，你会想象它们出自约翰·巴里摩尔或者林德伯格的手笔。”
“是，可它们怎么署名？”
她迟疑。“从来只有‘你的恋人’。”
“所以你刚看见我的时候，以为——”他起了个头，又停住，因为她脸红了。
“你收这些东西多久了？”
“从我记事起。我有两柜子的盒子。新的要么在我醒来时放在床边，要么在早餐时放在我的位子上。”
“可这个——人——怎么把盒子送到你这儿？他交给你姨母，她们放的？”
“我不确定。”
“可冬天他们怎么送？”
“我不知道。”
“听我说，”他给自己再倒些柠檬水，“你多久没去大陆了？”
“差不多十八年。姨母们告诉我，我出生在战争中期，在大陆上。”
“什么战争？”他吃惊地问，洒了些柠檬水。
“世界大战呗。怎么了？”
杰克·巴里盯着洒出的柠檬水，感到一种清醒人生里从未体验过的恐怖。他周围没有任何东西变了。他仍能感到同样的烈日晒在肩上，同样冰凉的杯子在手里，鼻端仍是同样的柠檬酸味。他仍能听见波浪轻微的劈拍。
可一切都变了，又暗又晕，像昏厥前瞥见的风景。所有细小的不谐音符突然聚成了焦点。因为柠檬水洒在了姑娘丢下的那份报纸的标题上，那标题写着：

在那颗大字黑横幅之下，游着几行小字：

===Original===

---
Suddenly he felt a surge of relief. He had noticed that the paper was yellow and brittle-edged.
“Why are you so interested in old newspapers?” he asked.
“I wouldn’t call day-before-yesterday’s paper old,” the girl objected, pointing at the dateline: July 20, 1933.
“You’re trying to joke,” Jack told her.
“No, I’m not.”
“But it’s 1953.”
“Now it’s you who are joking.”
“But the paper’s yellow.”
“The paper’s always yellow.”
He laughed uneasily. “Well, if you actually think it’s 1933, perhaps you’re to be envied,” he said, with a sardonic humor he didn’t quite feel. “Then you can’t know anything about the Second World War, or television, or the V−2s, or Bikini bathing suits, or the atomic bomb, or﻿—”
“Stop!” She had sprung up and retreated around her chair, white-faced. “I don’t like what you’re saying.”
“But﻿—”
“No, please! Jokes that may be quite harmless on the mainland sound different here.”
“I’m really not joking,” he said after a moment.
She grew quite frantic at that. “I can show you all last week’s papers! I can show you magazines and other things. I can prove it!”
She started toward the house. He followed. He felt his heart begin to pound.
At the white door she paused, looking worriedly down the road. Jack thought he could hear the faint *chug* of a motorboat. She pushed open the door and he followed her inside. The small-windowed room was dark after the sunlight. Jack got an impression of solid old furniture, a fireplace with brass andirons.
“Flash!” croaked a gritty voice. “After their disastrous break day before yesterday, stocks are recovering. Leading issues﻿ ﻿…”
Jack realized that he had started and had involuntarily put his arm around the girl’s shoulders. At the same time he noticed that the voice was coming from the curved brown trumpet of an old-fashioned radio loudspeaker.
The girl didn’t pull away from him. He turned toward her. Although her gray eyes were on him, her attention had gone elsewhere.
“I can hear the car. They’re coming back. They won’t like it that you’re here.”
“All right they won’t like it.”
Her agitation grew. “No, you must go.”
“I’ll come back tomorrow,” he heard himself saying.
“Flash! It looks as if the World Economic Conference may soon adjourn, mouthing jeers at old Uncle Sam who is generally referred to as Uncle Shylock.”
Jack felt a numbness on his neck.

===Chinese===

---
突然他涌起一阵宽慰。他注意到那报纸发黄、边角脆了。
“你怎么对旧报纸这么感兴趣？”他问。
“前天的报纸我才不叫旧，”姑娘反对，指着日期栏：1933年7月20日。
“你在开玩笑，”杰克告诉她。
“不，我没有。”
“可现在是1953年。”
“现在开玩笑的是你。”
“可报纸都黄了。”
“报纸总是黄的。”
他不安地笑笑。“好吧，如果你真以为现在是1933年，也许你倒值得羨慕，”他说，带着自己也不大有底的讥诮。“那你对第二次世界大战一无所知，对电视、V-2、比基尼泳装、原子弹，还有——”
“住口！”她跳起来，绕着椅子退开，脸色煞白。“我不喜欢你说的这些。”
“可是——”
“不，求你！在大陆上也许全然无害的玩笑，在这里听着就不一样了。”
“我真的不是在开玩笑，”过了一刻他说。
她这下真慌了。“我可以把上礼拜的报纸全给你看！我可以给你看杂志和其他东西。我能证明！”
她朝屋子走去。他跟上。他感到自己的心开始擂鼓。
在白门前她停住，忧虑地望着路那头。杰克觉得他能听见摩托艇隐约的突突声。她推开门，他跟她进去。小窗的房间在阳光之后显得暗。杰克得到一个印象：结实的旧家具，带黄铜柴架的壁炉。
“快讯！”一个沙哑的嗓音嘎声道。“继前天灾难性的暴跌之后，股市正在回升。龙头股……”
杰克发觉自己惊跳起来，不由自主地擟住了姑娘的肩。同时他注意到，那声音来自老式收音机的弧形棕色喇叭筒。
姑娘没有挣开。他转向她。虽然她的灰眼睛望着他，她的注意力已经去了别处。
“我听见汽车了。她们回来了。她们不会高兴你在的。”
“不高兴就不高兴吧。”
她更激动了。“不，你必须走。”
“我明天再来，”他听见自己说。
“快讯！世界经济会议看来即将闭幕，满口讥嘲山姆大叔——如今大家叫他夏洛克大叔。”
杰克感到脖颈一阵发麻。

===Original===

The room seemed to be darkening, the girl growing stranger still.
“You must go before they see you.”
“Flash! Wiley Post has just completed his solo circuit of the Globe, after a record-breaking flight of 7 days, 18 hours and 45 minutes. Asked how he felt after the energy-draining feat, Post quipped﻿ ﻿…”
---
He was halfway across the lawn before he realized the terror into which the grating radio voice had thrown him.
He leaped for the branch overhanging the fence, vaulted up with the risky help of a foot on the barbed top. A surprised squirrel, lacking time to make its escape up the trunk, sprang to the ground ahead of him. With terrible suddenness, two steel-jawed semicircles clanked together just over the squirrel’s head. Jack landed with one foot to either side of the sprung trap, while the squirrel darted off with a squeak.
Jack plunged down the slope to the rocky spine and ran across it, spray from the rising waves spattering him to the waist. Panting now, he stumbled up into the oaks and undergrowth of the first island, fought his way through it, finally reached the silent cove. He loosed the line of the *Annie O.*, dragged it as near to the cove’s mouth as he could, plunged knee-deep in freezing water to give it a final shove, scrambled aboard, snatched up the boathook and punched at the rocks.
As soon as the *Annie O.* was nosing out of the cove into the cross waves, he yanked up the sail. The freshening wind filled it and sent the sloop heeling over, with inches of white water over the lee rail, and plunging ahead.
For a long while, Jack was satisfied to think of nothing but the wind and the waves and the sail and speed and danger, to have all his attention taken up balancing one against the other, so that he wouldn’t have to ask himself what year it was and whether time was an illusion, and wonder about flappers and hidden traps.
When he finally looked back at the island, he was amazed to see how tiny it had grown, as distant as the mainland.
Then he saw a gray motorboat astern. He watched it as it slowly overtook him. It was built like a lifeboat, with a sturdy low cabin in the bow and wheel amidship. Whoever was at the wheel had long gray hair that whipped in the wind. The longer he looked, the surer he was that it was a woman wearing a lace dress. Something that stuck up inches over the cabin flashed darkly beside her.

===Chinese===

房间似乎在暗下去，姑娘愈发陌生了。
“你必须在她们看见你之前走。”
“快讯！怀利·波斯特刚刚完成他的环球单人飞行，以7天18小时45分钟刷新纪录。被问及这项耗尽体力的壮举之后感觉如何，波斯特俏皮地说……”
---
穿过草坪走到一半，他才意识到那刺耳的广播嗓音把他扔进了怎样的恐怖。
他跃向探出围栏的树枝，借一脚踩上带刺的顶端，冒险翻了上去。一只来不及逃上树干的松鼠吃了一惊，窜到他前头的地面上。两个钢牙的半圆带着骇人的突然，就在松鼠头顶上咣当合拢。杰克一脚落在已弹起的捕兽夹两侧，松鼠吱的一声窜远了。
杰克冲下坡去到岩石脊椎，跑着越过它，渐涨的浪花溅湿他到腰际。他喘着气，跌跌撞撞爬上头一座岛的橡树和灌木，一路披斩，终于抵达寂静的小湾。他解开《安妮·奥号》的缆绳，把它拖到尽可能接近湾口，蹚进齐膝的冰水给它最后一推，爬上船，抓起船钩撑向岩石。
《安妮·奥号》刚探出湾口迎上交叉的浪，他就猛地升帆。渐劲的风灌满它，送得单桅船侧倾出去，下风舷漫上几英寸的白水，向前冲刺。
有好一阵，杰克满足于只想风、浪、帆、速度和危险，让全部注意力忙于在它们之间找平衡，这样就不用问自己现在是哪一年、时间是不是幻象，不用琢磨飞来波女郎和暗藏的捕兽夹。
等他终于回头看那座岛，他惊讶它已缩得那么小，远得像大陆一样。
随后他看见船尾有一艘灰色摩托艇。他看着它慢慢追上来。它造得像一条救生艇，船首有座结实的低舱，舵在船中。掌舵的那人灰白长发在风里翻飞。他越看越确定那是个穿蕾丝裙的女人。她旁边有什么东西高出船舱几英寸，黑黢黢地闪。

===Original===

Only when she lifted it to the roof of the cabin did it occur to him that it might be a rifle.
But just then the motorboat swung around in a turn that sent waves drenching over it, and headed back toward the island. He watched it for a minute in wonder, then his attention was jolted by an angry hail.
Three fishing smacks, also headed toward town, were about to cross his bow. He came around into the wind and waited with shaking sail, watching a man in a lumpy sweater shake a fist at him. Then he turned and gratefully followed the dark, wide, fanlike sterns and age-yellowed sails.

===Chinese===

直到她把它举到舱顶，他才想到那可能是一支步枪。
可就在那时，摩托艇一转，转得浪头浇了它一身，朝岛折返回去。他惊奇地看了一分钟，随后一声怒喝撞进他的注意。
三条渔船，也正朝镇子去，眼看要横过他的船头。他转进风里，帆抖着等待，看一个穿疙瘩毛衣的男子朝他挥拳头。然后他转身，感激地跟上那三条深色的、宽大的、扇形的船尾和年久发黄的帆。
